FR-1.10Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic I — Copy & vocabularyTechnical Spec: Store Home (Home Tab) — Epic I — Copy & vocabulary
Card copy — the rule, and the terms it bans
  • Card copy is specified here, not in the Epic C catalogue. Three rules, in priority order:
  • 1. Name the consequence, then the action: The headline says what is or isn't happening to someone; the detail line and button say what the rep does about it.
  • 2. No internal state names: If a rep would not use the word out loud to another rep, it does not go on a card.
  • 3. Say who is affected: Customers, players, orders, or a named division — not "the system", not the passive voice.
  • Banned on cards — every one of these appeared in a first-draft condition name and would have shipped:
  • Do not write · Because · Write instead
  • Freight routing unresolved
    • Because: Describes a routing table, not a task
    • Write instead: Needs a manager before orders can ship
  • Below MOQ
    • Because: Acronym for an internal threshold
    • Write instead: Under the minimum to ship
  • Held by the render gate
    • Because: Pure internal machinery
    • Write instead: Still generating images
  • Unconfirmed application sizing
    • Because: Rep-facing term is just "sizing"
    • Write instead: Sizing needs confirming
  • Team re-bind / left-behind records
    • Because: Describes the data operation
    • Write instead: From the team you switched away from
  • Order-window coverage gap
    • Because: Analysis output, not a problem statement
    • Write instead: Some days have no team able to order
  • Submission blocked / cannot be submitted
    • Because: Passive, and names the block not the fix
    • Write instead: Add a manager so these orders can ship
  • Container
    • Because: Internal shorthand for a freight grouping
    • Write instead: Group
  • Primary, or Primary Contact (as a manager tag)
    • Because: "Primary" reads as a rank or manager type, implying a status field that doesn't exist; "Primary Contact" is a third term for something the product already names
    • Write instead: Point of Contact
  • Orphaned products / dangling category reference
    • Because: Describes the data state, not what the rep lost
    • Write instead: Products aren’t in a category
  • Say group, never container. Confirmed: the rep-facing word for a batch of orders consolidated into one freight shipment is group — as in the Orders tab's own Submit Group action. Container is internal shorthand and must not appear on Home, in the summary rail, or in any card copy. The prototype used it; that was an error.
Rep-facing headlines
  • Counts and names are interpolated. These are the headline patterns; detail lines follow the same rules.
  • ID · Headline · Detail line · Button
  • C-01
    • Headline: n pending orders to submit
    • Detail line: Oldest placed n days ago — orders reach the factory after you submit
    • Button: Go to Pending Orders
  • C-02
    • Headline: Division 3 is under the minimum to ship
    • Detail line: n more items needed, or submit as-is and a minimum-quantity fee applies
    • Button: View group
  • C-03
    • Headline: Division 3 and Division 7 need a manager before their orders can ship
    • Detail line: n orders are waiting on this
    • Button: Assign managers
  • C-04
    • Headline: Add a delivery address before orders can ship
    • Detail line: Orders ship to the team manager, and there's no address on file yet
    • Button: Add address
  • C-05
    • Headline: n players haven't ordered their required gear
    • Detail line: Window closes date — n of them have no email or phone, so a reminder won't reach them
    • Button: View players
  • C-06
    • Headline: n Roster Bank captures awaiting review
    • Detail line: Oldest captured n days ago — banked from storefront orders, not yet on a roster
    • Button: Open Roster Bank
  • C-07
    • Headline: n teams carry no products
    • Detail line: U10 Red, U14 White — there's nothing to buy for these teams yet
    • Button: Review coverage
  • C-08
    • Headline: n divisions or teams have no manager assigned
    • Detail line: Orders for them can't ship until someone's assigned
    • Button: Assign managers
  • C-09
    • Headline: Add a product before you launch
    • Detail line: Customers need at least one thing to buy
    • Button: Go to Products
  • C-10
    • Headline: n products are still generating images
    • Detail line: Customers can't see them until the images are ready
    • Button: View products
  • C-11
    • Headline: Images failed for n products
    • Detail line: They can't go on the storefront until this is retried
    • Button: Retry
  • C-12
    • Headline: n bundles have only one item left
    • Detail line: They've come off the storefront — add an item or delete the bundle
    • Button: Fix bundles
  • C-13
    • Headline: Confirm sizing for n products
    • Detail line: Sizing hasn't been confirmed on every decoration
    • Button: Confirm sizing
  • C-14
    • Headline: Ordering window closes date — n days left
    • Detail line: Change the close date from the store schedule
    • Button: Edit Store Schedule
  • C-15
    • Headline: n players still haven't ordered and the window closes in n days
    • Detail line: Send reminders where you have contact details, or move the close date
    • Button: View players
  • C-16
    • Headline: Your store is ready to launch
    • Detail line: Customers can start ordering as soon as you do
    • Button: Launch store
  • C-17
    • Headline: Your close report is ready
    • Detail line: Totals, fundraising, and what shipped
    • Button: View report
  • C-18
    • Headline: n orders are still being made
    • Detail line: They'll ship as they're finished — nothing needed from you
    • Button: View orders
  • C-19
    • Headline: Some days have no team able to order
    • Detail line: n days in the window aren't covered by any team's schedule
    • Button: Review schedule
  • C-20
    • Headline: n orders haven't been paid for
    • Detail line: Payment is still open on these
    • Button: View orders
  • C-21
    • Headline: n manager records are from the team you switched away from
    • Detail line: Keep them or remove them
    • Button: Review managers
  • C-22
    • Headline: Someone from the previous team can still see this store
    • Detail line: Name has access to this store's reports and rosters
    • Button: Review access
  • C-23
    • Headline: Fundraising is off, but n products still add to it
    • Detail line: This is fine if it's intentional
    • Button: View products
  • C-24
    • Headline: n invited managers haven't signed in yet
    • Detail line: Invited n days ago
    • Button: Review invites
  • C-27
    • Headline: n products aren't in a category
    • Detail line: A category they were in was deleted — shoppers can still buy them, but can't filter or search for them
    • Button: Re-file products
  • C-12 keeps the word bundle but drops "Incomplete": Bundle is rep-facing; Incomplete is a state name from Products Epic J. Verify whether reps see that state label on the Products tab — if they do, the card may use it; if it's internal, it stays off.
  • C-18, C-20, and C-23 deliberately reassure: All three are Informational conditions that a rep may have caused on purpose or cannot act on. Per Governing rule 3, Home reports and does not scold — "nothing needed from you" and "this is fine if it's intentional" are doing real work.
  • No card uses the word "blocked": It names the block rather than the fix, which is precisely the C-03 defect.
The tier is not displayed on the card — decided
  • The tier is not shown on the card. A prototype rendered a red BLOCKING eyebrow above C- 03; it was removed.
  • Tier is a spec-internal ordering property, and displaying it fails rule 2 above like any other internal term. If the headline names the consequence properly the eyebrow is redundant; if it doesn't, the eyebrow is compensating for weak copy. A red all-caps BLOCKING on something the rep may not have caused also sits badly against the never-scold rule. The rail colour carries severity for scanning, which is all it needs to do.
Product vocabulary
  • Home is a high-traffic surface and, if it becomes the landing tab, the rep's first exposure to the product's vocabulary on every visit. Its copy is therefore bound to the current labels and must be re-checked whenever they change — the same constraint and for the same reason as the Setup Guide's card copy.
  • Bound terms as of September 3, 2026: Stock Vault (not Product Vault), Catalog Templates (not Product Packages), Customizer (not Brand Catalog), Other stores, My Team(s), Roster Bank, Pending Orders, Submit (not approve), Open (not Active), Closed (not Paused / On Hold), Draft, Archived, Point of Contact (not Primary, not Primary Contact).
  • Status vocabulary is the lifecycle spec's, without exception: The canonical states are Draft, Open, Closed, Archived. Home must not render "Active" — a state that survives only in the stale Launch & Status Controls page. The workspace header's chip should be corrected on the same pass (Appendix A, 10).
  • Cards name the consequence, not the internal state: "Customers can't order these yet" over "visibility is off".
  • Cards never scold: No "you forgot", no "overdue", no red on an Informational condition. The rep is operating a store, not failing a checklist — the same tonal constraint the Setup Guide rewrite was driven by.
  • No completion, readiness, or score language anywhere on the page: Not in the attention list, not in the resume trail, not in the summary rail. This is a hard prohibition, inherited from the Setup Guide's "not a checklist" rule and extended here because Home is far more tempting to gamify.

  • 1. Name the consequence, then the action: The headline says what is or isn't happening to someone; the detail line and button say what the rep does about it.
  • 2. No internal state names: If a rep would not use the word out loud to another rep, it does not go on a card.
  • 3. Say who is affected: Customers, players, orders, or a named division — not "the system", not the passive voice.
  • Counts and names are interpolated. These are the headline patterns; detail lines follow the same rules.
  • The tier is not shown on the card. A prototype rendered a red BLOCKING eyebrow above C- 03; it was removed.
FR-1.11Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic J — Acceptance criteriaTechnical Spec: Store Home (Home Tab) — Epic J — Acceptance criteria
Epic J — Acceptance criteria
  • Given a rep opens a store workspace, then a Home tab is present and first in the navigation.
  • Given a store in any state, of any type, on any distribution format, then the Home tab is present and enabled — there is no configuration in which the workspace renders five tabs.
  • Given a store in any state, then the Needs your attention section and the In your store rail both render — neither is ever hidden, and neither is ever rendered disabled.
  • Given the In your store rail, then its first row is labelled My Team(s), matching the tab, and roster entries are counted as players.
  • Given any card, then no amber is used anywhere on Home.
  • Given the Setup Guide or Close Report trigger condition is met, when the rep enters the workspace, then that sequence presents over Home and Home is available on dismissal.
  • Given a store in any state, then every card in Needs your attention corresponds to a registered condition in Epic C and no card renders for an unregistered condition.
  • Given more than one condition resolves, then cards are ordered Blocking → Action → Informational, and identically on every render for the same store state.
  • Given more than five conditions resolve, then the top five render with an expander — unless more than five are Blocking tier, in which case all Blocking cards render.
  • Given a card is displayed, then its count matches the count on the owning tab exactly.
  • Given a card's primary action is selected, then the rep lands on the surface that owns that condition, scoped or filtered to the affected records where the owning spec supports it.
  • Given a condition's underlying state clears, when Home is next rendered, then that card is absent — and no card can be dismissed by any other means.
  • Given zero conditions resolve, then the section states so plainly and surfaces no substitute content.
  • Given an Archived store, then the attention section renders and states that the store is archived and read-only, and no card carries an action.
  • Given a Draft store with zero visible products, then C-09 renders at Blocking tier and Home does not offer any launch action.
  • Given a Draft store with at least one visible product, then C-16 renders and routes to the header launch control.
  • Given the rep performs a qualifying write, then a resume event is recorded against that rep, that store, and the surface's existing deep-link key.
  • Given a rep edits the same entity repeatedly, then one resume event is shown, timestamped at the most recent write.
  • Given a buyer order, a checkout write-back, a Roster Bank capture, or any system event, then no resume event is recorded.
  • Given two reps co-manage a store, then each sees only their own resume trail.
  • Given a resume row's target entity has been deleted, then the row is omitted with no error state.
  • Given the rep has no qualifying resume events, then the section is absent rather than empty.
  • Given any surface on Home, then no completion percentage, readiness score, or setup-progress indicator is displayed anywhere.
  • Given the Team Managers panel, then it lists the store's manager collection, and that collection is the same one Settings and Step 1 write.
  • Given at least one point-of-contact channel is on Team Manager mode, then the designated record is tagged Point of Contact — including when the collection holds a single record — and never tagged "Primary" or "Primary Contact".
  • Given only one channel is on Team Manager mode, then the tag names which channel it supplies.
  • Given both channels are on Salesperson or Custom, then no row is tagged, and the panel states who buyers actually see.
  • Given the rep wants to change which record is the point of contact, then Home routes to Settings → General and does not host the change.
  • Given a removal from Home would leave the collection with no designated record, then the removal is blocked with the reason stated and a route to Settings → General.
  • Given the manager modal is opened from Home, then it is the same modal Settings opens, with its full field set and validation and no Home-local variant.
  • Given the manager panel, then Add manager is the only add affordance — Add from Team Database is not offered on Home, and coach import routes to Settings → General.
  • Given any card, then its tier is not displayed as a label.
  • Given any registered condition, then it is false on a store with no outstanding work — no condition holds in the common case.
  • Given Add manager on Home, then the existing manager edit modal opens with its full field set and validation, and states that adding a manager grants no portal access.
  • Given a manager is added, edited, or removed from Home, then the result is indistinguishable from the same change made in Settings, and no portal invite is created, altered, or revoked.
  • Given a manager record with an active or pending invite is removed from Home, then the confirmation states that portal access remains until revoked in People & Access, and links there.
  • Given a point-of-contact channel is on Team Manager mode and the primary's contact details are edited from Home, then the storefront footer reflects the change without further action, and the modal stated so before saving.
  • Given the brand assets panel, then the Team Logo, Team Colors, and Store Theme Color render using the existing Step 1 / Settings components and the Prolook color picker, with no bespoke control — and all three stay editable after launch.
  • Given a manager row whose person holds an active or pending invite, then the row states that status rather than omitting the Invite action.
  • Given the theme color swatch, then its source is shown as Team default or Custom, a Reset to team color action is available on a custom value, and helper copy states the color applies to buttons and accents only.
  • Given the brand assets panel, then the Team artwork library is linked with its Team-level scope stated, and is not reproduced inline.
  • Given a League store, then the structure directory renders divisions with nested teams, standalone teams separately, and the same divisions · teams · players counts as the My Team(s) stats header, with divisions collapsed and the panel bounded in height.
  • Given an Individual Team store, then the structure directory does not render — and the Home tab renders unchanged in every other respect.
  • Given a division or team row is selected, then My Team(s) opens at that entity with the containing division auto-opened and breadcrumbs intact.
  • Given the structure directory, then no product thumbnails, coverage tables, catalog drawer, or team codes are rendered.
  • Given any card, then its headline names the consequence and its button names the action — no card headline states an internal system state, and no card uses a term from the banned list in Epic I.
  • Given any card, then its copy is the Epic I headline for that condition, not the Epic C internal condition name.
  • Given C-03, then the card names the specific affected divisions or teams rather than only a count.
  • Given any card, then every label it uses matches the label currently used by the owning spec — no card names a retired term.
  • Given Home renders, then no store state has been written by Home other than the rep-scoped resume event.
Next: FR-1.12
FR-1.12Governing rule 1 — Home is derived, and derives only from published stateTechnical Spec: Store Home (Home Tab) — Governing rule 1 — Home is derived, and derives only from published state
Governing rule 1 — Home is derived, and derives only from published state
  • This is the load-bearing constraint on the entire page and every future addition to it.
  • No new state: Home persists nothing about the store. It reads existing records and renders them. The one exception is the rep-scoped resume trail in Epic D, which is activity metadata about the rep, not state about the store.
  • Home may host an existing authoring component; it may never define one: This is the clause that permits the Team Managers panel (Epic F) and the brand assets panel (Epic G) to be editable without breaking the rule above. Where Home offers an edit, it opens the same component, writing the same persisted field, under the same validation as the surface that owns it. Home is a second doorway, not a second implementation.
    • The precedent is everywhere in this product, and the pattern has a name in the specs: one value, two surfaces: The structure editor is one component with two hosts (My Team(s) and League creation Step 2). The Add Products page is identical at Step 2 and on the Products tab. The ship-to destination address is one field shared by Step 1, the Fulfillment Method card, and the primary manager record — "never a wizard-local value copied forward." The announcement message is written by Step 1 and by Settings against the same store-level field with "no creation-time copy."
    • Three tests every Home-hosted editor must pass: It writes the field the owning spec owns, with no Home-local copy. It carries the owning spec's full validation and every consequence disclosure, unshortened. And a change made on Home is indistinguishable, afterward, from the same change made on the owning surface.
    • Where a component cannot pass those tests, Home routes instead of hosting: This is why Epic H renders the League structure read-only rather than embedding the structure editor: that editor already has two hosts and a documented rule that any change to it must be assessed against both. Adding a third host is a real cost, and a directory does not need one.
  • No second computation: Every count, threshold, and condition on Home is read from the surface that owns it. Home must not recompute "below MOQ", "days left", "n to submit", or any figure the Orders tab, the Active Order Window card, or the Reports tab already resolves. Where Home and a tab disagree, the tab is right and Home has a defect.
  • No new vocabulary: Home uses the label the owning spec uses. A rep must never learn a second name for a thing because they read about it on Home first. (This is the same constraint the Setup Guide carries under Naming in Tabbed Store Dashboard, Epic B, and for the same reason: Home is a high-traffic first-exposure surface.)
  • No enforcement: Home never blocks, never gates, and never becomes a prerequisite. Launch readiness is enforced by the launch gate (Store Status & Lifecycle, Epic A); freight readiness by the freight gate (Order Management, Epic B). Home describes those gates in the same language and must not duplicate, pre-empt, or contradict them.
  • Additions are amendments to Epic C, not to the component: A new attention condition is added by registering it in the Epic C catalogue with its owning spec, tier, trigger, and resolution route — the way a new modal is registered in System Modals & Toasts. A condition with no owning spec is not shippable.
FR-1.13Governing rule 2 — Severity tiersTechnical Spec: Store Home (Home Tab) — Governing rule 2 — Severity tiers
Governing rule 2 — Severity tiers
  • Home reuses the severity model already established for the modal layer in System Modals & Toasts, narrowed to three tiers and re-expressed for a persistent list rather than a modal. Tier drives ordering and treatment; it is a property of the condition, not of the rep's mood.
  • Tier · Definition · Treatment · Ordering
  • Blocking
    • Definition: Buyers, orders, or fulfillment are stopped right now. Someone outside the workspace is already affected.
    • Treatment: Red rail; action is a primary button
    • Ordering: First, always
  • Action
    • Definition: Rep work is queued. Nothing is broken, but something is waiting and has a cost if it keeps waiting.
    • Treatment: Blue rail; action is a secondary button
    • Ordering: Second
  • Informational
    • Definition: Time or state the rep should know. There may be no wrong answer.
    • Treatment: Neutral rail; action is a link or is absent
    • Ordering: Last
  • Tier is fixed per condition, not per store: A condition does not escalate because it has been there a while. Where urgency is genuinely time-derived, it is modelled as two separate conditions with different triggers (see C-14 and C-15), so each has its own copy and its own tier.
  • Home's rail palette is red, blue, and neutral. Amber is not used on Home at all: Decided. Two consequences follow, neither an objection.
    • Blue already means Draft: The status badge palette is Draft (blue) / Open (green) / Closed (amber) (Store Status & Lifecycle, Epic G). A blue Action card sitting near a blue Draft chip in the workspace header is the case to look at.
    • Amber means "gap" at the destinations Home routes to, and that mismatch is accepted: A team with no products renders amber on the My Team(s) canvas (Roster Management, Epic 6), and an unactivated invite shows an amber dot (Store General Settings, Epic A2b). C-07 and C-24 are therefore blue on Home and amber on arrival. The destinations are not being changed. What Home must never do is introduce a fourth colour of its own.
  • A Blocking condition always names who is blocked: "Customers can't order this yet", "pending orders can't be submitted" — the consequence, not the internal state.
FR-1.14Governing rule 3 — Guidance without a checklistTechnical Spec: Store Home (Home Tab) — Governing rule 3 — Guidance without a checklist
Governing rule 3 — Guidance without a checklist
  • "There needs to be more guidance" is how this product got a permanent setup checklist the first time, and how that checklist then got removed. The original guide sat permanently in the workspace tracking completion, which — in the owning spec's own words — made a one-time orientation problem into permanent furniture, and implied the store was incomplete long after it was fine. Its replacement over-corrected into a wall of text testers described as having too many words. Home is the third attempt at the same underlying request, and it will fail the same way unless the distinction below holds.
  • Guidance on Home means telling the rep what is true about their store right now. It never means telling them what a good store looks like.
  • Home does this · Home never does this
  • Reports a condition that currently holds: Lists steps that have not been taken
  • Names the consequence of that condition: Scores, ranks, or grades the store
  • Routes to the surface that resolves it: Implies a store is incomplete when it is fine
  • Says plainly when nothing needs the rep: Manufactures items to avoid an empty list
  • Explains what a tab is for when the rep lands there with nothing pending: Tracks whether the rep has visited a tab
  • Every item on Home is falsifiable: A card is on the page because a query returned a row. Remove the row and the card goes. Nothing on Home is an opinion about the store's quality, and nothing persists once its underlying state changes (Governing rule 1).
  • An empty attention list is the success state, not a gap to fill: A rep with a well-run store mid-window should see nothing. The temptation to fill that space with tips, checklists, or "next steps" is exactly the pressure that produced the removed checklist, and it is prohibited.
  • Orientation content stays where it lives: Explanations of what the Stock Vault is, what Team Required means, or how a product reaches the storefront belong to the Setup Guide, which is reopenable from Home (Epic B). Home links to it; Home does not reproduce it.
  • There is no first-visit-per-tab hint: A one-line "what this tab is for" statement on an empty attention list was considered and cut: it would require per-rep per-tab visit state — the only store-adjacent state Home would hold beyond the recent-work trail — and it is the seed of a checklist. Reassess against the empty-list metric if the empty state proves too bare.
FR-1.15Out of scopeTechnical Spec: Store Home (Home Tab) — Out of scope
Out of scope
  • Cross-store attention (a workspace-level "everything needing me across all my stores"), notifications or emails derived from attention conditions, snoozing or dismissing conditions, assigning a condition to another user, a full activity or audit log, per-rep configuration of which conditions appear, and any Home-side authoring of store state.
  • Cross-store attention is the obvious next feature and is deliberately excluded: It needs a different scope model, a different permission read, and a different ranking problem (which store's blocking condition wins?). Building it as a generalization of this page would compromise both. Note that the workspace-level Overview already exists as its natural host.
FR-1.16Metrics & Success CriteriaTechnical Spec: Store Home (Home Tab) — Metrics & Success Criteria
Metrics & Success Criteria
  • Share of Epic C conditions resolved within one session of first being surfaced: the core read on whether Home changes behavior or merely reports
  • Time from a Blocking condition first holding to its resolution, before and after Home, split by condition: the single number that justifies the feature
  • C-03 / C-04 dwell time: freight-blocked stores are the most expensive failure Home can catch; this should fall hardest
  • Share of store sessions where Home is the only tab visited: a hub that answers the question without a tab switch is working; a high rate alongside unresolved conditions means Home is a dead end
  • Click-through rate per condition: a condition nobody ever acts on is either wrongly tiered or does not belong in the catalogue
  • Cards rendered per session, distribution: a median above three suggests the tier model is too generous or the cap is too high
  • Share of sessions with an empty attention list: healthy and expected on well-run stores; near zero means Home is manufacturing work
  • Expander open rate: high means the cap is wrong; near zero means the cap is right and the collapsed conditions are correctly deprioritized
  • Resume-trail click-through rate: the honest test of whether continuity was a real problem or an assumed one. A low rate is a reason to cut the section, not to make it more prominent
  • Resume rows clicked by age: informs the expiry window
  • Resume-trail expander open rate, and the position of the clicked row: a rep regularly clicking row 8 means the visible count is too low; nobody opening the expander means five was right
  • Share of resume clicks targeting My Team(s): the tab testing named as the biggest friction should be the trail's heaviest consumer. If it isn't, the friction was confusion rather than navigation and the fix is elsewhere
  • Time from workspace entry to reaching a specific team or roster on League stores, before and after: the direct measure against "how often I got stuck on my teams"
  • Workspace re-entries per session: the behavior behind "I literally keep coming here". Home should either reduce the repeat landings or make them productive; if the count stays high and Home's click-through stays low, the landing surface is still empty in the way that matters
  • Manager records added from Home vs. from Settings vs. coach import: whether the panel is where reps actually go
  • Managers added from Home who are then invited to the portal within the same session: the read on whether the no-access-granting copy is landing. A low rate alongside support contacts about a manager "not being able to log in" means it is not
  • Theme colors and logos edited from Home vs. Settings, and share of stores accepting the team default
  • Structure directory rows clicked, by depth: division vs. team vs. standalone, and whether the scoped landing removes the re-navigation the "stuck on my teams" finding described
  • Send Reminder attempts on players with no contact channel: the failure C-05's detail line exists to pre-empt; it should fall as reps read the caveat before acting
  • Resume rows omitted for deleted targets: a high rate suggests the qualifying-write rules are too broad
  • Home / tab count disagreements reported: target zero, and the direct measure of Governing rule 1
  • Support contacts about "why can't I submit these orders": the C-03/C-04 pair's reason for existing
  • Support contacts about a bundle disappearing from the storefront: C-12's reason for existing
  • Stores launched with the rep having visited Home first vs. not, and time-to-launch for each: whether Home helps the Draft case the Setup Guide was not built to solve
  • Setup Guide reopens from Home vs. from the header: a read on whether the second entry point earns itself Dependencies & Technical Constraints
FR-1.17Dependencies & Technical ConstraintsTechnical Spec: Store Home (Home Tab) — Dependencies & Technical Constraints
Dependency & Technical Constraint
  • Home requires no new store state. It is a composition layer over existing records, and this is a constraint on the implementation as much as on the design: any figure Home renders must be sourced from the service that owns it rather than recomputed locally, or Home and the tabs will diverge and the page's credibility goes with them.
  • An attention-condition resolver is required: a single read, evaluated per store per render, returning the set of Epic C conditions currently holding, each with its count, its oldest-affected timestamp where applicable, its tier, and its resolution route. The resolver's predicates must be the owning services' own predicates — the pending-order query, the MOQ threshold evaluation, the freight-gate resolution, the render-state read, the Incomplete-bundle flag, the launch gate's visible-product count, the three-state fundraising read, the days-left derivation, the coverage-gap analysis, the invite-status read, and the re-bind source-team resolution. Home must not hold a second implementation of any of them. The condition catalogue is data, not code branches: adding a condition should be a registration, so that the "no unregistered condition renders" rule is enforceable rather than aspirational.
  • The resolver must be cheap enough to run on every workspace entry. Several predicates it composes are non-trivial on large stores — the roster↔order join behind C-05, the per-team product-coverage aggregate behind C-07, the catalog-wide fundraising read behind C-23. Home cannot be the surface that makes opening a store slow. Conditions are resolved progressively, with Blocking-tier predicates first, so the most consequential card is never the last to appear. Decided over synchronous resolution and short-TTL caching.
  • C-27 depends on an amendment to Products Tab that must land first. The orphaned state must be a first-class, queryable state owned by that spec, together with the Products-tab error treatment and an addressable filter for the affected items. Home may not scan for unresolved category ids itself, and must not ship ahead of that amendment: a registered condition whose owning surface has no query behind it is exactly the case Governing rule 1 prohibits.
  • Deep-link routing must exist for every resolution route in Epic C, and must reuse the existing schemes rather than adding one: Settings' section-key deep links (which must remain stable as sections are added), My Team(s)' division-scoped jump-ins including the auto-open of the targeted division, the Products catalog's scroll-to-and-focus return path, and the Orders tab's sub-tab and filter state. Where a route needs a filter Home cannot express — C-05's incomplete-player view, C-10's render-pending filter, C-23's exempted-item filter — that filter must be added to the owning tab as an addressable state, and that is an amendment to the owning spec rather than something Home implements locally. Audit which of those filters exist today and which are new work before estimating. This is the most likely source of hidden scope in the feature, and the only item on this page still carrying unknown cost.
  • The resume trail requires a new rep-scoped activity record: acting rep, store, surface key, optional entity reference, field-group or operation identifier, and timestamp — written on qualifying writes only, collapsed on read by surface + entity keeping the most recent, expired on read by age, and filtered to the requesting rep. Labels are composed at read time from the entity reference, so renames and edits are reflected and a deleted entity yields an omitted row rather than a dangling one. The write path must be explicitly opt-in per surface, not a blanket hook on every mutation — a general write hook would capture buyer orders, checkout write-backs, Roster Bank captures, render completions, and auto-closes, all of which are specified as non-qualifying. Bulk operations must emit one event referencing the operation rather than one per affected row.
  • Tab-label indicators need the resolver's per-tab roll-up, restricted to Blocking-tier conditions, and must express presence rather than count so that no figure lives in three places.
  • The Team Managers and brand assets panels require no new persistence. Both are additional hosts for components and fields that already exist: the manager collection with its single-primary invariant (writable from Settings, Step 1, and coach import, and now from Home, all against the same persisted collection), the Team Logo / Team Colors fields, and the store theme color field with its source flag. Three constraints follow. The manager modal must be the same component with unshortened validation and consequence disclosure, since a trimmed Home variant would drift. The no-access-granting rule must hold at the code path level — no route from Home may create, infer, or revoke a portal entitlement, and removal of a manager record must not touch the invite table. And the theme color's source flag must not be inferrable from the value, since a rep may pick the same color the team default would produce; Home reading or writing that field must preserve the flag so re-derivation on Change Team still behaves.
  • The League structure directory requires the shared division / team / roster state read in directory form — divisions with nested teams, standalone teams, per-entity player counts, and per-entity pending-capture counts, matching the figures the My Team(s) stats header and cards already resolve. It requires the existing scoped jump-in routes, including the auto-open of a targeted team's containing division. It requires no write path, and must not instantiate the shared structure editor, which already has two hosts and a rule that changes be assessed against both. Rendering must be bounded for large leagues so the directory cannot displace the attention list.
  • Store-state and store-type gating applies to card population, not to the tab or the section. The tab and the attention section always render; the resolver decides what the section contains. Gating belongs in the resolver rather than the view layer: Archived stores must return an empty set with an archived reason code (so the section can state its own emptiness rather than the view inferring it), League-only conditions must not be evaluated on Single-Team stores, and window-dependent conditions must not be evaluated on always-on stores. Gating in the view would leave conditions being computed for stores where they are meaningless and would eventually leak one.
  • Sequencing with the two paced card sequences requires no new state: Home reads neither the Setup Guide's per-rep-per-store-type dismissal flag nor the Close Report's per-user-per-close-event seen flag. Both sequences retain their existing triggers and precedence over Home. Home's Setup Guide link is a second caller of the existing reopen path.
  • Role scope depends on the open question above. If Home is ever extended to team-manager-role users, the resolver must return a permission-filtered condition set rather than the full set with disabled actions — a manager must never be shown a blocking condition they have no route to resolve.
  • Shared internal order, roster, catalog, store-config, and reporting services. Database updates limited to the resume-event record.
FR-2Roster Management (My Team(s) Tab)
  • Revision history — Jul 16, 2026: audited against the Team Stores Workspace prototype. Jul 28, 2026: tab renamed My Team(s); roster-level Upload / Clear / Delete actions moved into the roster toolbar. Aug 25, 2026: duplicate-number policy re-scoped to the roster level. Aug 31, 2026: league structure relabelled to divisions and teams; rosters made optional and never a launch prerequisite; Contact Email made optional; team codes moved to backend-only. Aug 31, 2026 (later): this tab's structure component is also used during League store creation (see Step 2 — Divisions & Teams (League Stores)), and the manager-mapping surface is named Settings → Distribution & Fulfillment throughout, matching the Settings spec. Aug 31, 2026 (later still): product visibility and assignment added to the League canvas and detail panels (Epic 6) — team nodes carry product thumbnails, detail panels gain Setup / Products tabs, and a docked catalog drawer assigns by click or drag. Oct 13, 2026: roster edits never mutate a placed order — the order line snapshots personalization at purchase, number changes stay unrestricted, divergence is reported, and pending orders can be updated to match (Epics 2 and 4). All of the above are written inline below — this list is a changelog, not a set of overrides.
  • The Problem: An accurate roster drives pre-filled personalization, per-player order tracking, reminder sends, and freight sorting by participant. But requiring the rep to assemble one — names, numbers, emails, sizes, addresses — before a store is useful is friction that research with high-value reps found unacceptable. Reps do not reject player tracking; they reject the roster as an upfront obligation. The roster's outputs are valuable; the roster as a gate is not.
    • Jersey numbers compound the problem. Whether two players may share a number is not a league-wide truth — a 6U division may not care while a varsity team does — so a single league-wide switch forces the rep to pick the wrong answer for some of their teams. And a number conflict discovered at submit is a conflict discovered too late: the player has already committed to a number that was never available.
    • Product assignment had the same shape of problem from the other direction. Assignment was authored on the Products tab, one product at a time, answering "which teams get this product?" — but the question a rep running a league actually asks is the reverse: "what does this team get, and which of my teams is missing something?" Answering it meant opening every product in turn and assembling the picture by hand.
  • The Solution: A My Team(s) tab inside the Team Store platform where store managers (reps, team managers, league directors) build and manage team structure, and populate rosters whenever they choose — before launch, during the ordering window, or not at all. A roster is never required to launch, and a team may exist with no roster attached.
    • Rosters fill from either direction. Front-load paths (manual entry, CSV import, share-roster template) are unchanged and remain available. Back-fill arrives from the storefront: team-gear orders create captures in the Roster Bank, which the rep reviews and commits onto a team's roster. Both paths write the same record.
    • Duplicate-number policy is set per roster, defaulted in bulk from the league level, and enforced by making unavailable numbers unselectable rather than by rejecting them after the fact.
    • For League stores the tab also answers the product coverage question in place: teams on the canvas show what they carry, gaps are visibly amber rather than silently empty, and products are assigned to a team or a whole division from a docked catalog drawer without leaving the structure view (Epic 6).
FR-2.1Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsUser StoriesTechnical Spec: Roster Management (My Team(s) Tab) — User Stories
User Stories
  • As a rep, I want to launch a store without building a roster first, so that I can go live immediately and let the roster fill in from orders.
  • As a rep running a league store, I want to organize teams into divisions that mirror my league's real structure, so that branding, product assignment, and order shipping all follow the same architecture.
  • As a rep, I want to create a team with no roster and add one later — or never — so that structure and player data are independent decisions.
  • As a rep or team manager, I want to add a player manually, so that I can quickly input a small team or make singular late adjustments.
  • As a rep or team manager, I want to download a template, populate it, and re-upload it, so that I can quickly import a large roster of players.
  • As a rep, I want to email the roster template to my team managers and share the team store link with selected players, so that roster-building and buying can start without me doing the data entry.
  • As a rep, I want to add a player record without an email or phone number, so that a roster I can only partly fill is still worth building.
  • As a rep or team manager, I want to edit any field of an existing player's record, so that I can correct spelling or update contact details.
  • As a rep, I want to fix a player's jersey number even after they have ordered, so that a typo is a two-second correction rather than something the system refuses.
  • As a rep, I want to be told when a player's ordered number no longer matches their roster number, so that I find out from the roster rather than from a parent holding the wrong jersey.
  • As a rep who catches a number mistake before anything ships, I want to push the correction onto the pending order, so that the right jersey gets made.
  • As a rep running a league, I want to set the duplicate-number rule per team rather than once for the whole league, so that a division that allows sharing and one that forbids it can coexist.
  • As a rep, I want to set the rule for every team at once and then adjust the exceptions, so that per-roster control doesn't mean configuring twenty rosters by hand.
  • As a rep, I want to turn duplicates off on a roster that already has a shared number, so that tightening the rule going forward isn't blocked by history I can't change.
  • As a rep or parent picking a number, I want unavailable numbers to be unselectable, so that I never choose a number only to be told at submit that I can't have it.
  • As a rep or team manager, I want to remove a player from the active roster, so that their jersey number is released back into the pool of available numbers.
  • As a rep or team manager, I want to clear an entire roster or delete a roster outright — with a confirmation step — so that I can restart bad data entry or remove a roster created by mistake.
  • As a rep running a league, I want to see at a glance which products each team carries, so that I can answer "what does this team get?" without opening every product.
  • As a rep, I want teams with no products called out visually, so that a coverage gap is something I notice rather than something a parent discovers.
  • As a rep, I want to assign a product to a team — or to an entire division at once — without leaving the structure view, so that fixing a gap is a drag rather than a detour.
  • As a rep, I want to inspect and edit a product from here, so that spotting a wrong price on a team's strip does not mean navigating to another tab and finding it again.
FR-2.2Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic 0 — League structure: divisions & teamsTechnical Spec: Roster Management (My Team(s) Tab) — Epic 0
Epic 0 — League structure: divisions & teams
  • In a League (Multi-Team Hub) store the My Teams tab organizes structure into divisions — e.g. a city or club, carrying one set of branding (name, color, sport) — which contain teams (the individual squads / age divisions). Teams may also stand alone, outside any division. Every team must have a name; a roster is optional on every team. This structure is shared platform-wide: product assignment (Products tab) and pending-order shipping containers (Orders tab) key off it.
  • Home view: Given a League store, when the My Teams tab opens, then it shows a stats header (divisions · teams · players), a canvas of division headers and team nodes, a standalone-teams list, and New Team / New Division actions. Team nodes and division headers carry the product affordances specified in Epic 6.
  • Single-Team Locker: Given a Single-Team store, then no division tier and no grouping UI is shown — the My Team tab drops straight into the team's player table.
  • Create / edit / delete: divisions are created with a name and brand color and edited in a modal (including fulfillment-manager mode); teams can be added to a division or standalone, renamed, and deleted with confirmation. Deleting a division keeps its teams — they become standalone teams, dropping the division's branding, and each keeps its roster and players intact.
  • This component is also used during League store creation. The same structure editor renders as Step 2 of League store creation — see Step 2 — Divisions & Teams (League Stores) — with branding and the team code omitted from the detail panel in that context. It is one component with two hosts, not two editors: behavior specified here applies in both, and structure built during creation is indistinguishable from structure built in this tab. Any change to this editor must be assessed against both surfaces.
    • Product affordances are workspace-only. The Epic 6 surfaces — thumbnail strips, count pills, the Products tab of the detail panel, and the catalog drawer — are not rendered in the creation step, because the store has no catalog until the Add Products step that follows. The creation-context detail panel shows Setup content only, with no segmented tabs.
  • Drill-in: opening a division lists its teams; opening a team shows breadcrumbs and a branded banner (division color, team avatar) above the player table. In the workspace the detail panel is split into Setup and Products tabs (Epic 6). Each division and team carries a deterministic, human-readable team code (e.g. RT47A).
  • Team codes are a backend key. Codes identify a division or team for order routing, container assignment, and reporting. They are not entered by the rep and are not entered by the shopper — the storefront resolves a shopper to a team with a team selector at arrival (see the Roster-Optional Architecture spec, Epic D). Codes remain visible in the workspace for reference and support, and are not surfaced at all during store creation.
  • Fulfillment mapping: each division routes orders to the league manager, a division manager, or individual team managers. Mapping is configurable in Settings → Distribution & Fulfillment (the Order Fulfillment Mapping, specified in Store General Settings, Epic A3) and directly on the division and team detail components; both surfaces read and write the same state. Division and team views surface an Order Fulfillment strip showing the effective manager or a "Not assigned" state with a Manage link — unassigned managers gate pending-order submission (see the Order Management spec).
Next: FR-2.3

FR-2.3Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic 1 — Filling the rosterTechnical Spec: Roster Management (My Team(s) Tab) — Epic 1
Epic 1 — Filling the roster
  • A team's roster can be filled by any combination of the paths below, at any point in the store lifecycle, plus Roster Bank commits (Epic 5). Front-loading and back-filling coexist on the same roster. The manual and CSV paths are also available during League store creation (Step 2 — Divisions & Teams, Epic C) for reps who prefer to configure everything upfront; records created there are ordinary player records.
  • Add manually (1-by-1). An inline row at the foot of the player table ("Add Player") containing all player data fields.
    • Given the add row, when it opens, then it contains all player data fields from the schema above (the address field appears only for Direct-to-Home stores). Only First and Last Name must be completed.
    • Number entry respects the roster's policy: the Jersey Number control follows Epic 4 — on a roster that disallows duplicates, already-assigned numbers are presented locked and unselectable; the inline error remains as a backstop for direct entry.
    • Given a valid record, when I confirm the row (green check), then the record is added to the roster table instantly.
Roster data import (batch CSV)
  • On-page entry: Upload Roster is a primary button in the roster view's toolbar, beside Clear Roster, Delete Roster (where offered), and the sort control.
  • Given the Upload Roster modal, when it opens, then it offers a drag-and-drop / click-to-browse zone and a "Download CSV template" link — a structured .csv skeleton with headers matching the data model.
  • Per-row validation with skip semantics: Given an uploaded file, when the parse engine validates rows, then errors are reported per row and cell (e.g. "Row 12: Missing last name", "Row 4: Duplicate jersey number 8 (also Row 2)"); invalid rows are skipped ("n rows skipped — fix and re-upload") while valid rows import, with a preview table and valid/skipped counts before confirming.
  • Only missing required fields skip a row. A row lacking an email or phone number imports normally. Email and phone are validated for format only when a value is present — a malformed email skips the row, an absent one does not.
  • Duplicate-number reporting on import is policy-aware: a duplicate number is a skip-worthy error only on rosters that disallow duplicates. On a roster that allows them, duplicate numbers import without error.
  • Share Roster Template: a standalone flow emails the blank roster template (prolook_roster_template.csv) to team managers so they can fill in player details and return it for import; the message is pre-drafted and signed by the logged-in rep. Manager email addresses come from the manager records in Settings, not from the roster, so this flow is unaffected by players having no contact details.
Next: FR-2.4

FR-2.4Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic 2 — Managing & editing player recordsTechnical Spec: Roster Management (My Team(s) Tab) — Epic 2
Epic 2 — Managing & editing player records
  • Edit: Given a player row, when I open its 3-dot row menu and choose Edit, then a side drawer opens pre-populated with the player's current details; the jersey-number control follows the roster's duplicate policy (Epic 4), with the player's own current number always selectable.
  • Delete: a single player is removed from the row menu instantly; bulk delete — via row checkboxes and the selection bar — shows a confirmation modal before removing the selected players. Removed jersey numbers are freed back into the available pool and become selectable again immediately.
  • Clear Roster: Given the roster view, when I click Clear Roster, then a confirmation modal states how many players will be removed and that the roster itself is kept; on confirm, all players are removed (numbers released) and a success toast confirms ("Roster cleared — n players removed"). The roster entity survives and remains attached to its team. Disabled when the roster is already empty.
  • Delete Roster: Given a roster in a League store, when I click Delete Roster, then a confirmation modal states the roster and its n players will be permanently deleted; on confirm the roster entity is removed and its team survives with no roster attached, the view returns to the team detail, and a success toast confirms ("Roster "name" deleted"). Not offered on a Single-Team store's only roster — use Clear Roster there. Deleting a team is a separate action in Epic 0 and removes the team along with any roster it holds.
  • Neither action touches the Roster Bank. Captures are not roster members — they live in the Bank until a rep commits them (Epic 5). Clear Roster and Delete Roster affect committed player records only; pending captures for that team remain in the Bank and are still available to commit. Confirmation-modal counts state committed players only, and where captures are pending the modal says so.
  • Neither action touches product assignments. Clearing or deleting a roster leaves the team's assigned products (Epic 6) untouched — products belong to the team, not to its player list.
  • Share Team Store Link: the row menu copies a per-player store invite link; selecting multiple players surfaces a bulk Share Team Store Link modal with recipients pre-filled from the selected players' contact + associated emails. Players with no email contribute no recipient and are listed as copy-only in the modal; where no selected player has an email, the modal offers the link for manual distribution rather than a send.
  • Team Gear column: each row shows the player's team-gear completion ("n/m team gear items") and opens their gear drawer — ordered items vs. missing required gear, with Send Reminder (see the Order Management spec). Send Reminder is disabled, with a reason, for a player with no contact channel.
  • Sorting: a toolbar sorts the table by Name (A–Z / Z–A) or Number (low→high / high→low), with a live player count.
Editing a player who has already ordered
  • Reps have been explicit that they would rather live with occasional bad data than be locked out of simple corrections. That preference governs the roster. It does not extend to orders, because a changed order is not a recoverable annoyance — it is a garment nobody can wear.
  • Personalization edits are never blocked by order state. Given a player with one or more team-gear orders, when the rep edits Jersey Number or Name on Jersey, then the edit saves with no block, no confirmation modal, and no warning interstitial. A rep correcting a typo meets no friction whatsoever.
  • The edit does not reach the order. Personalization is snapshotted onto the order line at purchase and is never re-read from the roster afterward (owned by Order Management). Changing the roster changes the roster; the factory still makes what was bought.
  • Divergence is reported where the rep already looks. Given a player whose ordered personalization differs from their current roster values, when the Team Gear column and the player's gear drawer render, then the affected item states both — ordered as #12 · roster says #7. This is passive information, not an error state and not a task: the rep may have changed the number precisely because the player's situation changed, and the system has no way to know which value is "right".
  • While the order is still pending, offer to fix it. Given an affected order that has not yet been submitted to the factory, when the divergence line renders, then it carries a single action — update the order to #7 — which rewrites that order line's snapshot to the current roster values. The pending window already exists for MOQ batching (Order Management, Epic B), so this reuses a genuine window rather than inventing one, and it covers the common case of a typo caught before production.
  • Once the order is submitted, the action is withdrawn and the information stays. Given a submitted order, then the divergence line renders without an action. The rep is told what will arrive so they can warn the parent or arrange a replacement; nothing in the product pretends it can still be changed.
  • Updating the order is per order line, never bulk. Each affected line is updated on its own action. A bulk "sync roster to orders" control is deliberately not offered — it would make a single mis-click capable of rewriting many paid orders at once, which is the one outcome this whole section exists to prevent.
  • Divergence never gates anything. It does not block submission, does not affect the launch gate, does not appear as a Home attention card, and does not change the player's team-gear completion count — the item was ordered, and it stays ordered.
    • Why no Home card: the condition would clear when the order is submitted, which is exactly when the rep loses the ability to act on it. A card vanishing at that moment reads as resolution and would be actively misleading.
Next: FR-2.5

Screenshot placeholder — Edit player side drawer
//
  • As a rep or team manager, I want to edit any field of an existing player's record, so that I can correct spelling or update contact details.
  • As a rep, I want to fix a player's jersey number even after they have ordered, so that a typo is a two-second correction rather than something the system refuses.
  • Edit: Given a player row, when I open its 3-dot row menu and choose Edit, then a side drawer opens pre-populated with the player's current details; the jersey-number control follows the roster's duplicate policy (Epic 4), with the player's own current number always selectable.
  • Personalization edits are never blocked by order state. Given a player with one or more team-gear orders, when the rep edits Jersey Number or Name on Jersey, then the edit saves with no block, no confirmation modal, and no warning interstitial. A rep correcting a typo meets no friction whatsoever.
  • The edit does not reach the order. Personalization is snapshotted onto the order line at purchase and is never re-read from the roster afterward (owned by Order Management). Changing the roster changes the roster; the factory still makes what was bought.

Screenshot placeholder — Row menu delete and bulk-delete confirmation modal (selection bar)
//
  • As a rep or team manager, I want to remove a player from the active roster, so that their jersey number is released back into the pool of available numbers.
  • Delete: a single player is removed from the row menu instantly; bulk delete — via row checkboxes and the selection bar — shows a confirmation modal before removing the selected players. Removed jersey numbers are freed back into the available pool and become selectable again immediately.
FR-2.4.3
Screenshot placeholder — Clear Roster confirmation modal
//
  • As a rep or team manager, I want to clear an entire roster or delete a roster outright — with a confirmation step — so that I can restart bad data entry or remove a roster created by mistake.
  • Clear Roster: Given the roster view, when I click Clear Roster, then a confirmation modal states how many players will be removed and that the roster itself is kept; on confirm, all players are removed (numbers released) and a success toast confirms ("Roster cleared — n players removed"). The roster entity survives and remains attached to its team. Disabled when the roster is already empty.
  • Neither action touches the Roster Bank. Captures are not roster members — they live in the Bank until a rep commits them (Epic 5). Clear Roster and Delete Roster affect committed player records only; pending captures for that team remain in the Bank and are still available to commit. Confirmation-modal counts state committed players only, and where captures are pending the modal says so.
  • Neither action touches product assignments. Clearing or deleting a roster leaves the team's assigned products (Epic 6) untouched — products belong to the team, not to its player list.

Screenshot placeholder — Delete Roster confirmation modal
//
  • As a rep or team manager, I want to clear an entire roster or delete a roster outright — with a confirmation step — so that I can restart bad data entry or remove a roster created by mistake.
  • Delete Roster: Given a roster in a League store, when I click Delete Roster, then a confirmation modal states the roster and its n players will be permanently deleted; on confirm the roster entity is removed and its team survives with no roster attached, the view returns to the team detail, and a success toast confirms ("Roster "name" deleted"). Not offered on a Single-Team store's only roster — use Clear Roster there. Deleting a team is a separate action in Epic 0 and removes the team along with any roster it holds.

FR-2.5Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic 3 — Direct-to-Home Delivery: player shipping addressTechnical Spec: Roster Management (My Team(s) Tab) — Epic 3
Epic 3 — Direct-to-Home Delivery: player shipping address
  • Applies only when the store's fulfillment is Direct to Home Delivery (chosen in Step 1 — Basics). In this scenario each player needs a shipping destination, but the rep is not required to know it up front.
  • Conditional field & column: Given the store uses Direct to Home Delivery, when the My Team(s) tab renders, then the add/edit form exposes a Player Shipping Address input and the roster table shows an additional Player Address column. For all other fulfillment models the field and column are not shown.
  • Optional entry: Given the address field, then it is optional for the rep — the record can be saved without it (mirrors Name on Jersey).
  • Map-service validation: Given a rep enters an address, when the record is saved, then the address is validated against a map service on the backend before it is accepted — an inline "Verifying address with map service…" state shows during the check, and a failed check offers a retry-or-leave-blank error.
  • Checkout write-back: Given a player record already exists and the rep left the address blank, when the parent/player enters their shipping address at storefront checkout, then that address writes back onto the roster and is reflected in the Player Address column with a "From checkout" badge; blank addresses show an "Awaiting checkout" placeholder.
  • Write-back vs. capture: write-back completes a record that already exists. Where no matching player record exists, the order does not write back — it creates a Roster Bank capture instead (Epic 5). These are the two directions of the same plumbing and must not both fire for one order.
  • Write-back is the one order → roster path, and it is not reversible. Checkout writes an address onto the roster; nothing in this tab writes an address back onto a placed order. A rep correcting a shipping address after an order exists is correcting the roster for next time — the existing order ships to the address it was placed with, and the divergence treatment in Epic 2 applies to the Player Address column in the same way it applies to personalization.
Next: FR-2.6

FR-2.6Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic 4 — Duplicate-number policy & number selectionTechnical Spec: Roster Management (My Team(s) Tab) — Epic 4
Epic 4 — Duplicate-number policy & number selection
  • Whether two players may share a jersey number is a per-roster decision, not a league-wide one.
Scope
  • The roster owns the policy. Each roster carries its own Allow Duplicate Numbers setting, set in the roster view and governing that roster only. A roster's own value is what validation reads — always.
  • The league-level control is a bulk default, not an enforcement layer. The Allow Duplicate Numbers control in Settings → Team & Roster (Roster Rules card) behaves as a select-all / bulk apply: setting it at the league level cascades through every division to every team's roster in the store. Any roster may then override it, and the league-level control never overrides a roster afterward.
  • The same league-level value can be set before the store exists. Step 1 — Basics, Epic D1 surfaces the Allow Duplicate Numbers control during store creation, writing the same store-level field the Settings card reads. It is one value with two entry points, not a creation-time copy.
  • New rosters inherit the league-level default at creation, then diverge freely. This includes rosters created during League store creation (Step 2 — Divisions & Teams, Epic C), which read the value set in Step 1.
  • Mixed states are normal and must be legible. Where rosters disagree with the league-level default, the Settings control reports a mixed state rather than implying uniformity, and each roster view states the policy currently in force — so a rep never has to open Settings to understand why a number was accepted or rejected.
When duplicates are NOT allowed — lock, don't flag
  • Taken numbers are locked and unselectable. Given a roster that disallows duplicates, when the rep or a shopper reaches jersey-number entry, then numbers already assigned on that roster render disabled and non-selectable. The conflict is prevented at selection, not reported at submit.
  • Uncommitted Roster Bank captures hold their numbers. The taken-number set for a roster is the union of its committed player records and the pending captures banked against that team. A number claimed by a capture nobody has committed yet is unavailable — otherwise two buyers could take the same number in the gap between order and commit.
  • Every number-entry surface obeys this: the roster add row, the edit drawer, the storefront PDP jersey-number entry, and checkout. A number that cannot be had must never appear selectable anywhere.
  • Number grid at selection. Number entry presents the roster's full number range as a grid with taken numbers greyed out, so the picker chooses from what is genuinely available instead of guessing. The player's own current number stays selectable when editing their record.
  • The inline error becomes a backstop. "Number [X] is already assigned to [Player Name]" still fires, but only for paths that bypass the picker — CSV import and direct field entry. It is no longer the primary mechanism.
  • Releasing a number through player delete, bulk delete, or Clear Roster returns it to selectable immediately. A number held by a pending capture is released when that capture is dismissed from the Roster Bank, not by clearing the roster.
When duplicates ARE allowed
  • Numbers remain fully selectable and no conflict error fires. Pending captures impose no constraint.
  • A number already in use is labelled "duplicate" rather than "taken" — on a roster where sharing is deliberate, "taken" reads as a blocker and misleads the picker.
Turning duplicates off on a roster that already has one
  • Tightening the policy is a forward-looking decision, and the rep making it usually cannot undo the history that preceded it — two players may have ordered the same number already, and a jersey in production cannot be un-numbered.
  • The switch is never blocked. Given a roster carrying one or more shared numbers, when the rep turns Allow Duplicate Numbers off, then the setting saves. No validation sweep runs, no conflict list is presented, and the rep is not asked to resolve anything first.
  • Existing duplicates are grandfathered. Given a shared number assigned while duplicates were allowed, when the policy is turned off, then those assignments stand. No record is altered, no number is released, and no order is affected.
  • The new rule governs new selections only. Given the policy is off, when any number-entry surface renders, then the taken-number set and the locking behavior above apply from that point on. A grandfathered number counts as taken, so no third player can join it.
  • The roster reports the exception rather than asserting a rule that is currently false. Given a roster carrying grandfathered duplicates, when the roster view renders, then it states the condition and its count — duplicates off · 2 players share #7, assigned before the rule changed — with the affected rows identifiable. This is the same three-state honesty the product already applies to fundraising disabled-with-exemptions (Store General Settings, Epic A4) and to the Roster Rules mixed state.
  • The exception clears itself. Given a grandfathered duplicate, when one of the sharing players is deleted, moved, or renumbered, then the condition resolves with no further action. Nothing prompts the rep to resolve it, and nothing is gated while it stands.
  • Turning duplicates back on clears the exception reporting and restores ordinary shared-number behavior.
Next: FR-2.7

FR-2.7Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic 5 — The Roster Bank in the My Team(s) tabTechnical Spec: Roster Management (My Team(s) Tab) — Epic 5
Epic 5 — The Roster Bank in the My Team(s) tab
  • Team-gear orders create captures — potential roster entries that wait for a rep to commit them. The Bank's capture, pre-grouping, and commit behavior is specified in the Roster-Optional Architecture (Open Access Stores) spec, Epic C. This section covers only where it lives in this tab and how it interacts with the surfaces above.
  • Two entry points, one queue. The Roster Bank is available per team — from the team detail view, showing that team's pending captures — and as a store-wide view from the My Team(s) home, showing all pending captures with a team column and filters. Both read the same records; committing from either has the same effect.
  • Counts surface where the rep already looks. Team and division cards, and the store-wide stats header, show a pending-capture count alongside the player count so a rep sees unreviewed captures without opening the Bank.
  • Committing writes a normal player record. A committed capture becomes an ordinary row on that team's roster and is indistinguishable from a manually entered one thereafter. If the team has no roster yet, committing the first capture creates one.
  • Captures are not players. They do not appear in the player table, are not counted in player counts, and are not affected by Clear Roster or Delete Roster (Epic 2) — but they do hold jersey numbers (Epic 4) and do count toward team-gear completion tracking.
  • A capture commits at the values it was captured with. Committing writes the personalization the buyer entered at checkout, so a freshly committed record never diverges from the order that created it. Divergence (Epic 2) can only arise from a rep edit afterward.
  • Not present during store creation. The Bank has nothing to hold before a storefront exists, so it is not surfaced in the League creation step (Step 2 — Divisions & Teams, Epic C).
Next: FR-2.8

FR-2.8Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic 6 — Product visibility & assignment (League stores)Technical Spec: Roster Management (My Team(s) Tab) — Epic 6
Epic 6 — Product visibility & assignment (League stores)
  • League stores only. A Single-Team Locker has one team and no coverage question — the store's catalog is that team's catalog — so none of the surfaces below render there. They are also absent during store creation (Epic 0), where no catalog exists yet.
  • Assignment itself is not redefined here. The catalog record, its team setting, its pricing, and the team-code bindings it carries are owned by the Products tab spec. This Epic specifies a second, structure-first surface onto that same state: the Products tab answers "which teams get this product?", and this tab answers "what does this team get, and who is missing something?" Both read and write the same assignments, and neither is authoritative over the other.
On the canvas
  • Team product strip: Given a League store, when the canvas renders, then each team node carries a strip of product thumbnails showing what that team currently carries.
  • Gaps are amber, not empty: Given a team with no assigned products, when its node renders, then the strip shows an amber "No products yet" state rather than blank space. A team carrying nothing is a condition to notice, not an absence to overlook.
  • Per-node add: each team node carries a + that opens the catalog drawer scoped to that team (see Add from Catalog below).
  • Division count pill: Given a division header, when it renders, then it shows a product count pill for the division — the number of products reaching its teams, whether assigned division-wide or team-by-team.
Inspecting a product without leaving the tab
  • Hover card: Given a product thumbnail on a team node, when the rep hovers it, then a floating product card appears carrying the same content as the Products-tab product card — thumbnail, product name, design ID, team setting, assigned teams, and Youth / Adult prices. It is the same card component, not a lookalike.
  • Full detail in a modal: Given a product thumbnail, or a product row title in the detail panel, when the rep clicks it, then the full product detail page opens in a modal over the tab. Editing and saving work from there and write to the catalog record directly — a wrong price spotted on a team's strip is corrected without navigating to the Products tab and finding the product again.
  • Closing the modal returns the rep to the canvas or panel they opened it from, with any edit reflected immediately in the strip and cards.
Detail panel — Setup / Products tabs
  • The division and team detail panels are split by a segmented control: Setup and Products.
  • Setup holds the existing panel content — roster, the Order Fulfillment strip, rename and delete.
  • Team detail → Team Products tab. Given a team, when the Products tab opens, then it lists the products assigned to that team, with rows sourced from a division-wide assignment marked "via division" so the rep can tell inherited coverage from coverage assigned to this team specifically. The tab carries an Add Products split menu — Add New Product (hands off to the existing add-product flow) and Add from Catalog (opens the drawer) — and a Roster button jumping to that team's player table.
  • Division detail → Division Products tab. Given a division, when the Products tab opens, then it shows the division-wide products list plus a per-team coverage table — every team in the division and what it carries. Teams with no coverage show an amber "+ Assign products" action inline, so the fix is offered at the point the gap is visible. The tab carries Add Products and Add Team.
  • "+ Add Product" also sits in the tab header, so the action is reachable without opening the split menu.
Add from Catalog — the docked drawer
  • Docks, does not navigate: Given Add from Catalog from any entry point, when it opens, then the catalog docks as a right-hand drawer beside the canvas or panel. The rep never leaves the structure view, so the thing they are assigning to stays visible while they choose.
  • Finding a product: the drawer offers search and three filters — All, Unassigned (products on no team), and Not on [X] (products absent from the launching team or division, named explicitly).
  • Click to assign: Given a product in the drawer, when the rep clicks Assign, then it is assigned to the launching scope — the team or division whose + or Add Products opened the drawer.
  • Drag to assign anywhere: Given the grip on a drawer product, when the rep drags it onto any team node, then it is assigned to that team — not only the launching scope. Dragging onto a division header creates a division-wide assignment covering all of its teams, which those teams surface as "via division" rows.
  • Immediate feedback: an assignment updates the target's thumbnail strip, the division count pill, and the coverage table without a reload, and clears any amber gap state it resolves.
  • Division-wide assignment extends forward. A product assigned to a division applies to every team in that division, including teams added to the division after the assignment was made. No re-assignment step is required when a team is added, and the assignment is not recalculated or confirmed at the point a team joins.

  • As a rep running a league, I want to see at a glance which products each team carries, so that I can answer "what does this team get?" without opening every product.
  • As a rep, I want teams with no products called out visually, so that a coverage gap is something I notice rather than something a parent discovers.
  • Team product strip: Given a League store, when the canvas renders, then each team node carries a strip of product thumbnails showing what that team currently carries.
  • Gaps are amber, not empty: Given a team with no assigned products, when its node renders, then the strip shows an amber "No products yet" state rather than blank space. A team carrying nothing is a condition to notice, not an absence to overlook.
  • Per-node add: each team node carries a + that opens the catalog drawer scoped to that team (see Add from Catalog below).
