FR-2.9Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic 7 — Move a player to another teamTechnical Spec: Roster Management (My Team(s) Tab) — Epic 7
Epic 7 — Move a player to another team
  • Given a player row in a roster, when the rep opens the row menu and chooses Move to team, then a destination picker opens listing every other team in the store. In League stores the list is grouped by division and is searchable.
  • Given selection mode with one or more players selected, when the rep chooses Move to team, then the same picker opens and applies to the whole selection.
  • Given a destination, when the rep confirms, then each player leaves the origin roster and joins the destination roster. The player's jersey number is released on the origin roster and becomes selectable there immediately.
  • Given a player's jersey number, when that number is already taken on the destination roster or is disallowed by the destination roster's duplicate-number policy, then the rep is prompted to assign a different number or to move the player without one. The taken-number set at the destination follows Epic 4 — committed player records and pending Roster Bank captures both count.
  • Given a player with one or more team-gear orders, when a move is attempted, then the move is refused, the control is disabled, and the reason is stated. In a bulk move, blocked players remain on the origin roster and the result names each one.
  • Product assignment does not travel with the player. A moved player inherits the destination team's assigned products (Epic 6) and loses access to products assigned only to the origin team.
  • Moving the last player off a roster leaves the roster entity in place, as Clear Roster does (Epic 2). The roster is not deleted by becoming empty.
  • OPEN — the order-state block above should be reconsidered. It is now the only hard block in this tab, and it sits against the governing rule at the top of this page: every other roster edit is permitted and its consequence reported. Under the snapshot model there is no data reason to refuse a move — the existing order keeps its own personalization and is unaffected. There is, however, a real operational reason: that order is routed to the origin team's shipping container, so it will ship to the old coach whichever roster the player now sits on, and freight arriving at the wrong destination is a materially worse outcome than a mismatched number. Two candidate resolutions: keep the refusal, or permit the move and state the freight consequence plainly on the confirmation. Recommend the latter, but it is a fulfillment decision rather than a roster one and should be confirmed with the Orders spec before changing.
  • To confirm before build: whether a move is permitted once the store is Active or Closed, and whether moves are recorded in the store's activity history.
Next: FR-2.10
FR-2.9.1
Screenshot placeholder — Move to team destination picker
//
  • Given a player row in a roster, when the rep opens the row menu and chooses Move to team, then a destination picker opens listing every other team in the store. In League stores the list is grouped by division and is searchable.
  • Given selection mode with one or more players selected, when the rep chooses Move to team, then the same picker opens and applies to the whole selection.
  • Given a destination, when the rep confirms, then each player leaves the origin roster and joins the destination roster. The player's jersey number is released on the origin roster and becomes selectable there immediately.
  • Given a player's jersey number, when that number is already taken on the destination roster or is disallowed by the destination roster's duplicate-number policy, then the rep is prompted to assign a different number or to move the player without one. The taken-number set at the destination follows Epic 4 — committed player records and pending Roster Bank captures both count.
  • Given a player with one or more team-gear orders, when a move is attempted, then the move is refused, the control is disabled, and the reason is stated. In a bulk move, blocked players remain on the origin roster and the result names each one.
  • Product assignment does not travel with the player. A moved player inherits the destination team's assigned products (Epic 6) and loses access to products assigned only to the origin team.
  • Moving the last player off a roster leaves the roster entity in place, as Clear Roster does (Epic 2). The roster is not deleted by becoming empty.
FR-2.10Governing rule — permissive about the roster, immutable about the orderTechnical Spec: Roster Management (My Team(s) Tab) — Governing rule
Governing rule — permissive about the roster, immutable about the order
  • The roster is the rep's working list. An order line is a manufacturing instruction somebody has paid for. These are different kinds of object and they get different treatment.
  • Roster edits are never blocked by order state. A rep may change a jersey number, a name on jersey, or a duplicate-number policy at any time, including on a player who has already ordered. Reps have been explicit that an over-restrictive system costs them more than the occasional data error, and the errors in this area are low-stakes and visible.
  • A roster edit never mutates a placed order. Personalization is snapshotted onto the order line at purchase and is never re-read from the roster afterward (see Order Management). Changing a roster row changes the roster, not what the factory makes.
  • Divergence is reported, not prevented. Where the two disagree, the tab says so plainly at the point the rep is already looking, and — while the order is still pending — offers to bring the order into line.
  • The cost of a mistake decides the treatment. Two players sharing a number is a recoverable annoyance and is permitted. A submitted order silently changing its personalization is an unwearable garment and a chargeback, and is prevented by the snapshot rather than by a gate on the rep.
FR-2.11Object modelTechnical Spec: Roster Management (My Team(s) Tab) — Object model
Object model
  • The My Team(s) tab manages four distinct things. The distinction between a team and its roster matters: a team can exist and function with no roster at all.
  • Entity · Applies to · Notes
  • Division
    • Applies to: League stores only
    • Notes: The grouping tier — typically a city or club. Carries branding: name, color, sport. Contains teams. Optional; a league store may have none. Can hold division-scoped product assignments inherited by its teams (Epic 6).
  • Team
    • Applies to: All stores
    • Notes: The unit everything else keys off: product assignment, shipping containers, fulfillment mapping, and the team code. A team sits inside a division or stands alone under the league. Every team must have a name.
  • Roster
    • Applies to: All stores
    • Notes: The player list. Optional — a team may have no roster. Once created, a roster is attached to exactly one team, and one team holds at most one roster. There are no free-standing rosters.
  • Player record
    • Applies to: All stores
    • Notes: A row on a roster. Schema below.
  • In a Single-Team Locker store there is no division tier and no grouping UI: the store is one team, and the My Team tab drops straight into that team's roster.
Each player record must contain the following defined schema
  • Only First Name and Last Name are required. A record may carry no contact channel at all — reps frequently do not hold parent contact details, and requiring one would reintroduce the upfront burden this tab exists to remove. Contact fields are validated for format when supplied and left empty otherwise.
  • Field · Required · Type / Validation · Notes
  • First Name
    • Required: Required
    • Type / Validation: String, max 50 characters
  • Last Name
    • Required: Required
    • Type / Validation: String, max 50 characters
  • Jersey Number
    • Required: Optional
    • Type / Validation: Integer, 00–99
    • Notes: Uniqueness depends on that roster's Allow Duplicate Numbers setting (Epic 4). Where duplicates are disallowed the number must be unique on that roster, taken numbers are locked at selection, and real-time conflict checking backstops any direct-entry path. Where duplicates are allowed, no uniqueness constraint applies. 2-digit numeric input. Editing this field never alters an order already placed — the ordered number is snapshotted on the order line (Epic 2).
  • Name on Jersey
    • Required: Optional
    • Type / Validation: String, max 15 characters
    • Notes: Converted to UPPERCASE dynamically. Snapshotted onto the order line at purchase, as Jersey Number is.
  • Contact Email
    • Required: Optional
    • Type / Validation: Valid email format, validated only when provided
    • Notes: Used for parent/guardian communication and share-link recipient pools where present. A record saves without one. Most commonly arrives from a storefront capture rather than from rep entry.
  • Contact Phone Number
    • Required: Optional
    • Type / Validation: String, validated to a 10-digit numeric format
    • Notes: Validated only when provided — a record saves without a phone number.
  • Player Shipping Address
    • Required: Optional
    • Type / Validation: Street, city, state, ZIP; validated against a map service on the backend
    • Notes: Present only when the store's fulfillment is Direct to Home Delivery (selected in Step 1 — Basics of the store-creation flow). Optional for the rep to enter — if left blank, the parent/player supplies it at storefront checkout and it writes back onto the roster (same pattern as Name on Jersey). Not collected at all for other fulfillment models.
  • Associated Emails
    • Required: Optional
    • Type / Validation: List of valid emails
    • Notes: Additional parent/guardian emails linked to the record; included in share-link recipient pools alongside the primary contact email where one exists.
  • Records with no contact channel. A record carrying neither an email nor a phone number is valid and fully functional for personalization, number assignment, product scoping, and freight sorting. Two behaviors degrade and must show it: Share Team Store Link offers copy-only (no send) for that player, and Send Reminder is disabled with a reason. Roster Bank captures usually supply a contact channel, so records committed from orders typically have one even when rep-entered records do not.
FR-2.12Metrics & Success CriteriaTechnical Spec: Roster Management (My Team(s) Tab) — Metrics & Success Criteria
Metrics & Success Criteria
  • Time from store-creation start to launch, and share of stores launching with no roster
  • Share of League stores whose structure was built during creation vs. in this tab — a read on whether the creation step is carrying its weight
  • Roster completeness at ordering-window close — committed players vs. estimated true team size
  • Roster edit → storefront latency
  • CSV import success rate; rows skipped per upload
  • Manual add → save completion without duplicate-number errors
  • Duplicate-number errors surfaced at submit — target near zero once numbers are locked at selection; a non-trivial rate means a number-entry surface is bypassing the picker
  • Share of rosters overriding the league-level default — a read on whether per-roster scope was worth building, and on whether the bulk default is set to the right value
  • Personalization edits made against players who already have orders, and the share that produce a divergence — the frequency of the case Epic 2 exists for
  • Share of divergences resolved through the pending-order update, and the share discovered only after submission — if most are caught too late, the divergence line is not visible enough or the pending window is shorter in practice than it looks
  • Time from a divergent edit to the pending-order update — how much of the pending window reps actually use
  • Rosters carrying grandfathered duplicates, and how long the condition persists — a read on whether reps tidy it up or live with it, which is the direct test of the permissive policy
  • Roster Bank health — pending captures remaining at window close, and one-click-commit share vs. manual split/merge
  • Share of committed records carrying a contact channel — a read on how much reminder and share coverage the emergent path actually delivers
  • Manager assignment completeness per division and team (freight-gate readiness for pending-order submission)
  • Share-link and team-selector → order conversion
  • Zero accidental clears/deletes (confirmation-gated); clear/delete actions tracked
  • Structure-editor defects reported against only one of its two hosts (this tab, League creation Step 2) — target zero
  • Teams launching with zero assigned products — the gap the amber state exists to prevent; expected to fall toward zero
  • Product assignments made from this tab vs. the Products tab — a read on whether the structure-first surface matches how reps actually think
  • Drawer assignments by method — click-Assign vs. drag, and division-header drops as a share of drags
  • Product detail modal opens by entry point (hover card, thumbnail click, panel row title), and edits saved from the modal
  • Time to answer "which of my teams is missing products", before and after the coverage table
FR-2.13Dependencies & Technical ConstraintsTechnical Spec: Roster Management (My Team(s) Tab) — Dependencies & Technical Constraints
Dependency & Technical Constraint
  • Requires genuine team-database integration: roster sync and member/role management. The division / team / roster architecture must be persisted as shared platform state — product assignment (Products tab), pending-order shipping containers (Orders tab), and the Settings manager mapping all read the same structure. Team and roster are separate entities: a team may exist with no roster, a roster attaches to exactly one team, and a team holds at most one roster. Division deletion must re-home teams as standalone rather than deleting them, carrying their rosters with them. Clear Roster must release all jersey numbers and preserve the roster record; Delete Roster must remove the roster entity while preserving its team; team deletion must remove the team from shared state, which product assignment and shipping containers consume. Neither clear nor delete may destroy pending Roster Bank captures or product assignments.
  • Order lines snapshot personalization; roster records do not feed them at read time. This is a constraint on the order model (owned by Order Management) that this tab depends on absolutely: jersey number, name on jersey, size, and shipping address are copied onto the order line at purchase, and no roster mutation may ever reach a placed order. Without the snapshot, every permissive behavior specified above becomes dangerous rather than convenient. The divergence read compares a player's current roster values against the snapshot on each of their order lines and is consumed by the Team Gear column and the gear drawer; it must be a derived comparison rather than a stored flag, so it resolves itself when either side changes. The pending-order update action rewrites one order line's snapshot from the current roster values and is available only while that order remains in the pre-submission pending state defined by Order Management, Epic B; it must be per line, with no bulk variant, and must be refused server-side once the order is submitted rather than merely hidden in the UI.
  • The structure editor must be a single shared component with two hosts — this tab and Step 2 — Divisions & Teams (League Stores) — parameterized to suppress branding, the team code, and all Epic 6 product affordances in the creation context without forking behavior. Suppression is presentational only: team codes are still generated deterministically at creation, and division brand color still takes its default. Structure created in the wizard writes to this same shared state, committed atomically with the store on deploy.
  • Product visibility and assignment (Epic 6) requires the catalog ↔ team assignment join read in the team→products direction, not only product→teams as the Products tab reads it, with per-team and per-division aggregates driving the thumbnail strips, the division count pill, and the coverage table. Assignment scope must be modelled at two levels — team and division — so an inherited assignment can be rendered as a "via division" row and distinguished from a direct one; the inheritance rule for teams added to a division after a division-wide assignment is open (see Epic 6) and determines whether division scope is a stored rule or a drop-time fan-out. The product card and product detail page must be the same components the Products tab uses, rendered here as a floating card and a modal respectively — a second implementation would guarantee drift in card content, pricing display, and edit behavior. Saving from the modal writes the catalog record directly and must invalidate the strips, pills, cards, and coverage tables reading it. The catalog drawer requires catalog search, the three filter predicates (all / unassigned / not-on-scope, the last parameterized by the launching team or division), a launching-scope binding for click-Assign, and drag-and-drop assignment whose drop targets are team nodes and division headers on the canvas — writing the same team-code bindings the Products tab writes, with optimistic UI reconciled against the server result. Assignment permissions follow the Products tab's existing rules; this surface adds no new authority.
  • Manager mapping needs per-division fulfillment modes (league / division / individual teams), manager records (name, email, 10-digit phone, address), and backend address verification shared with the freight gate. Mapping state must be writable from both Settings → Distribution & Fulfillment and the division/team detail components without divergence.
  • Team codes are backend routing and reporting keys. They must be generated deterministically per division and team, stamped onto orders for container routing and reporting, and exposed read-only in the workspace. No rep-facing or shopper-facing code entry exists; shopper→team resolution happens through the storefront team selector (Roster-Optional Architecture, Epic D).
  • Duplicate-number policy must be persisted per roster, with the league-level Settings control implemented as a bulk write cascading through divisions to every roster plus a mixed-state read — not as a parent value that rosters inherit at read time, since a roster's own setting is authoritative once set. New rosters read the league-level default at creation only; that league-level field is the same one written by Step 1 — Basics Epic D1, with no separate creation-time storage. Number entry requires a per-roster taken-number set — the union of committed player records and pending Roster Bank captures for that team — exposed to every selection surface (roster add row, edit drawer, storefront PDP, checkout) so unavailable numbers can be rendered disabled rather than validated after submit. The set must exclude the edited player's own current number and must invalidate on player delete, bulk delete, Clear Roster, capture commit, and capture dismissal. Validation remains as a server-side backstop for CSV import and direct field entry, and duplicate-number import errors must be evaluated against the target roster's policy rather than a league-wide rule.
  • Turning duplicates off must not trigger a retroactive validation pass. The policy write is a simple field update with no sweep, no conflict resolution step, and no mutation of existing records. The grandfathered-duplicate read is derived — a per-roster query for numbers held by more than one committed record while the policy is off — and drives the roster view's exception line only. It is a report, never a gate, and must resolve automatically when the underlying records change rather than requiring a dismissal.
  • Field-level validation must enforce the schema (string max-lengths, jersey integer 00–99, valid email when provided, 10-digit phone when provided), with First and Last Name as the only required fields and no requirement that a record carry any contact channel. Surfaces that depend on a contact channel — Send Reminder, share-link sends, player-addressed system emails — must degrade to a disabled or copy-only state with a stated reason rather than blocking record creation.
  • The batch path needs a CSV template generator and a parse/validation engine returning per-row / per-cell error reporting with skip-and-import semantics (invalid rows skipped, valid rows imported) and an import preview; both the manual and CSV paths must be reachable from the League creation step as well as this tab. Deleting a record must release its jersey number back into the available pool. Sharing requires an email channel for the roster template and store-link sends, per-player invite-link generation, and an associated-emails join for recipient pools; the Team Gear column requires the same roster↔order join used by the My Team(s)-tab gear drawers.
  • The Roster Bank must be readable both per team and store-wide from this tab, with pending-capture counts joined onto division cards, team cards, and the stats header; capture storage, pre-grouping, and commit mechanics are specified in the Roster-Optional Architecture spec. Shared internal team services + database updates.
FR-3Customize New Product
  • The Problem: A rep clicking Add Products → Customize New Product currently proceeds without brand context enforced. In a multi-brand environment this creates licensing exposure — a rep could list product from a brand their dealership is not authorized to sell — and a bloated experience, since brands irrelevant to their entitlement are still visible. Separately, starting a custom product from a blank slate is overwhelming; reps need guided entry by sport and category.
  • The Solution: One brand context, resolved from QX7 and expressed in the SOURCE pill on the Add Products page. That selection does two things: it scopes which saved designs and saved carts appear under the Customizer source chip, and it determines which brand customizer opens when the rep clicks Customize New Product. From there the rep enters a sport/category picker scoped to that brand, hands off to the brand customizer, and returns with a staged custom product.
  • Who is this for? Reps adding new custom products to a store — both during store creation (Step 2 – Products) and on an existing store's Products tab. Indirectly relevant to Prolook admins, who own brand entitlement in QX7 but do not act inside this flow.
  • Scenarios encountered: Starting a new uniform from scratch for a store; entering the flow as a single-brand rep and going straight into the customizer; picking between two entitled brands before designing; switching to a second entitled brand partway through building a catalog, to add gear the first brand doesn't carry; opening a bookmarked customizer link for a brand the dealership has since lost access to; landing in the flow with no entitlements configured at all; browsing saved designs and carts scoped to the brand currently on the pill; picking a sport and category to reach the right design template; returning from the customizer with a finished item and configuring it before commit.
FR-3.1Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsTechnical Spec: Customize New Product — Functional Requirements & Core Workflows
User Stories
  • As a rep entitled to one brand, I want to land straight in that brand's customizer when I click Customize New Product, so that the common path stays fast.
  • As a rep entitled to several brands, I want to see only the brands I can actually sell, so that I pick the right one without wading through catalogs I have no access to.
  • As a rep entitled to a licensed brand, I want it to appear alongside owned and manufactured brands, so that I can offer licensed product.
  • As a rep, I want the brand I've selected to govern both the saved designs I browse and the customizer that opens, so that I'm working in one brand context rather than tracking two.
  • As a rep, I want to switch to a different entitled brand mid-session without losing what I've already staged, so that building a multi-brand catalog isn't a start-over.
  • As a rep, I want to be blocked from reaching a brand I'm not entitled to even by direct URL, so that I can't accidentally list something my dealership isn't authorized to sell.
  • As a rep whose access to a brand has been removed, I want a clear access-denied message rather than a silent failure, so that I understand why a link that used to work no longer does.
  • As a rep whose dealership has no entitlements configured, I want an explicit state telling me who to contact, so that I don't assume the tool is broken.
  • As a rep, I want to pick a sport and category to start, so that I enter the right design template instead of a blank slate.
  • As a rep, I want the customizer to open already carrying the store's team name, colors, and logos, so that I'm not re-entering branding the store already knows.
Acceptance Criteria — Brand resolution
  • Given a rep opens the Add Products page, when the page loads, then the SOURCE pill resolves against QX7 and offers only the brands that rep's dealership is entitled to sell.
  • Given QX7 returns exactly one entitled brand, when the page loads, then the pill resolves to that brand automatically and no picker is shown.
  • Given QX7 returns two or more entitled brands, when the rep clicks Customize New Product before resolving the pill, then they are required to choose a brand before a customizer opens.
  • Given QX7 returns no entitled brands, when the page loads, then the pill and the Customizer source chip render an explicit state naming the problem and directing the rep to their Prolook admin, while the Stock Vault, Catalog Templates, and Other stores chips continue to function normally.
Acceptance Criteria — Scope of the selection
  • Given a brand is resolved on the pill, when the rep filters to the Customizer source chip, then only that brand's saved designs and saved carts are returned.
  • Given a brand is resolved on the pill, when the rep clicks Customize New Product, then that brand's customizer opens.
  • Given a brand is resolved on the pill, when the rep filters to Stock Vault, Catalog Templates, or Other stores, then results are unchanged by the brand selection.
Acceptance Criteria — Switching and enforcement
  • Given a rep has resolved the pill to one brand, when they reopen the picker, then their prior selection is visible but not pre-committed and they must explicitly re-select.
  • Given a rep switches the pill to a different entitled brand, when the switch commits, then the Customizer chip and the customizer target re-scope to the new brand and any already-staged items remain staged and unaffected.
  • Given a rep navigates directly to a brand customizer or catalog route their dealership is not currently entitled to, when the request is made, then the server returns an access-denied response rather than catalog data.
Acceptance Criteria — Picker and hand-off
  • Given the rep has reached the sport/category picker, when it renders, then only sport and category templates belonging to the resolved brand are shown.
  • Given the picker, when the rep selects a sport/category card, then they are handed off to that brand's customizer on the matching design template.
  • Given the customizer opens from this flow, when it loads, then Team Name, Team Colors, and Logos are pre-populated from the store's branding.
  • Given the rep finishes a design, when they return from the customizer, then the item is staged as a custom product and configured through the standard per-item configuration modal, which is the last point at which its design can be changed.
Next: FR-3.2
FR-3.2Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic A — Brand entitlement & the SOURCE pillTechnical Spec: Customize New Product — Epic A
Epic A — Brand entitlement & the SOURCE pill
  • A1 — The brand list is entitlement-filtered from QX7: The pill's Change link opens a Choose a brand picker listing only brands the rep's dealership is entitled to sell. There is no fixed brand list; the count varies per user.
  • A2 — Single-entitlement reps never see the picker: Where QX7 returns exactly one brand, the pill resolves to it automatically and Customize New Product opens that brand's customizer with no intermediate step.
  • A3 — Multi-entitlement reps select explicitly: Where QX7 returns two or more brands, the rep must resolve the pill before Customize New Product will open a customizer. Brand type (owned/manufactured vs. licensed) is shown where QX7 exposes it.
  • A4 — Scope of the selection: The pill governs the Customizer source chip and the Customize New Product button only. Stock Vault, Catalog Templates, and Other stores are not brand-scoped and are unaffected by it.
  • A5 — Server-side enforcement: Direct navigation to a brand's customizer or catalog the dealership is not currently entitled to is blocked server-side and returns an access-denied response, not data. Hiding the UI affordance is not sufficient. Enforcement must ship with the UI change, not after.
  • A6 — Brand switching within a session: The rep can reopen the picker and choose a different entitled brand at any point. The prior selection is visible but not pre-committed. Switching re-scopes the Customizer chip and the customizer target; already-staged items are unaffected.
  • A7 — Zero-entitlement state: Where QX7 returns no brands, the pill and the Customizer chip render an explicit state naming the problem and directing the rep to their Prolook admin — not a silent empty list. The rest of the Add Products page continues to function, since the other three sources do not depend on brand.
  • A8 — Both entry points behave identically: Step 2 of store creation and the Products tab of an existing store enforce A1–A7 the same way.
Next: FR-3.3

FR-4Launch & Status Controls
  • Audit update — July 16, 2026: this spec was audited against the current Team Stores Workspace prototype and updated to match its behavior — including the launch gate, the launch confirmation/success modals, and the post-launch header (Preview Store button + Create Template menu).
  • The Problem: Reps need an explicit, reversible action to take a store live and to pause it.
  • The Solution: Every new store begins as a draft so the rep can fully configure it before launching. A Launch control — gated on the store having at least one product added — flips the store open through a confirmation modal and a success modal (View Store / Stay In Editor). After launch, the workspace header swaps to a Preview Store button (opens the live storefront) and a store menu with a Create Template action; status is surfaced on each dashboard store card, while the actionable pause/resume control lives on the Settings tab's Store Status card (Close / Re-open).
  • Who is this for? Store owners / reps
  • Scenarios encountered: Going live after setup; previewing the live storefront from the workspace header; creating a reusable template from a proven store; checking a store's Draft / Open / Closed state from its dashboard card; pausing a store; confirming a store is open.
FR-4.1Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsTechnical Spec: Launch & Status Controls — Functional Requirements & Core Workflows
User Stories
  • As a rep, I want explicit launch and close controls, so that I command exactly when buyers can order.
  • As a rep, I want to preview my live storefront and spin a reusable template off a launched store straight from the workspace header, so that I can verify what buyers see and reuse a proven setup.
Acceptance Criteria
  • Draft on creation: Given a rep creates a new store, when it is created, then it starts as a draft — deliberately not live — so the rep can fully configure it before launching.
  • Gate — one product added: Given a draft store with zero products in its catalog, when the rep views the workspace header, then LAUNCH STORE is disabled with the tooltip "Add at least one product to launch your store." Basics completeness is enforced upstream by the creation wizard's per-step validation, and no roster gate applies at launch. (updated — the gate is products added, not published; "published" is no longer a valid term, having been replaced by the per-item visibility toggle)
  • Launch confirmation modal: Given an enabled Launch control, when clicked, then a "Launch Team Store" modal confirms the consequences — all players and the team manager receive an email that the store was created and can purchase team goods, and the store can be closed at any time — with Cancel / Launch Team Store actions.
  • Launch: Given the confirmation, when the rep confirms, then the store goes open, a "Your store is now live!" success modal offers View Store or Stay In Editor, and the onboarding milestone advances.
  • Live header controls: Given a launched store, when the workspace renders, then the LAUNCH STORE button is replaced by a Preview Store button that opens the live storefront (same behavior as the former View Store link) and a store menu whose single option, Create Template, spins a reusable store template off this store.
  • Status semantics: Given a launched store, then it can be Open or Closed — Closed suspends ordering (technically equivalent to a draft) but, unlike a never-launched draft, flips back to Open directly. Status changes confirm with toasts, and the actionable pause/resume control lives on the Settings tab's Store Status card (Close Store / Re-open Store — see the Store General Settings spec), not in the workspace header. (updated — this state was previously named On Hold, and the live state was previously named Active; Open and Closed are the names used throughout, per Store Status & Lifecycle)
  • Status surfaces: Given the status state, then it is displayed as a Draft / Open / Closed tag on each dashboard store card, and the Stores tab's Filter control offers Open, Draft, and Closed as filter values; changing status anywhere reflects everywhere. Archived is a fourth status, but archived stores are excluded from the directory and their cards appear only in the Archived Stores section of the Account page. (updated)
Next: FR-4.2

FR-4.2Metrics & Success CriteriaTechnical Spec: Launch & Status Controls — Metrics & Success Criteria
Metrics & Success Criteria
  • Launch success rate
  • Accidental-launch reversals near zero
  • Launches blocked by the product gate (and time to first product added)
  • Storefront previews from the header; templates created from launched stores
FR-4.3Dependencies & Technical ConstraintsTechnical Spec: Launch & Status Controls — Dependencies & Technical Constraints
Dependency & Technical Constraint
  • Requires store status persistence (draft / open / closed / archived) and propagation to the storefront (open/closed behavior). The launch gate reads the store's catalog count (≥1 product added); confirming launch triggers a notification send to all rostered players and the team manager and fires the onboarding-milestone event. The post-launch header requires a storefront preview route and a create-template flow seeded from the store's configuration. Status is a shared read model consumed by dashboard-card tags and the Stores-tab filter, so every surface stays in sync; the pause/resume write path lives on the Settings tab's Store Status card. Database updates.
FR-5Store Status & Lifecycle
  • Executive Summary & Problem Statement
    • The Problem: Store status governs buyer access, order intake, workspace permissions, and reporting across the entire platform — but the rules defining what each state means, how transitions occur, and what changes per surface are scattered across individual UI epics rather than defined in one authoritative place.
    • The Solution: A single source of truth for all four store states — Draft, Open, Closed, and Archived — covering their definitions, valid transitions, behavioral consequences per surface, and acceptance criteria. All other features reference this section for status semantics; they own only the UI surface that triggers a transition.
  • User persona & use cases
    • Who is this for? Store owners (reps) managing store availability; Prolook support agents handling archive reversals; buyers who encounter a closed or archived storefront URL.
    • Scenarios encountered: Setting up a new store in Draft before it's ready to go live; attempting to launch without any products added; manually closing a store mid-season to make catalog changes; a pop-up store auto-closing when its ordering window closes; extending a window by re-opening; permanently archiving a store at season end; a buyer landing on a closed storefront URL.
FR-5.1Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic A — Draft state & launchTechnical Spec: Store Status & Lifecycle — Epic A
Epic A — Draft state & launch
  • As a rep, I want my new store to start as a draft so I can configure it fully before any buyers can see it.
  • As a rep, I want to be prevented from launching a store with no products, so I don’t accidentally go live with an empty catalog.
  • Default state on creation: Given a rep completes the store creation wizard and deploys, when the store is created, then it enters Draft state. The storefront is not live and no buyer-facing URL is accessible.
  • Full workspace access: Given a Draft store, when the rep opens the management workspace, then all tabs (Detail, Products, Roster, Orders, Reports) are accessible and fully editable — the rep can configure everything before launch.
  • Launch gate — product required: Given a Draft store with zero products, when the rep attempts to launch, then the launch action is disabled and a clear message explains that at least one product must be added before the store can open.
  • Launch gate satisfied: Given a Draft store with at least one product added, when the rep clicks Launch Store, then the store transitions to Open immediately and the storefront becomes publicly accessible.
  • One-way transition: Given a store that has been launched and is now Open, Closed, or Archived, then the Draft state is not available as a target — there is no “revert to draft” action anywhere in the UI.
  • Draft badge in stores list: Given a Draft store, when it appears in the Stores tab list, then it shows a Draft badge and is excluded from the Open Stores KPI count.
  • Draft is stated at first entry: Given a newly created Draft store, when the rep enters the workspace for the first time, then the Store Setup Guide's cover card states that the store is not yet live — see Tabbed Store Dashboard, Epic B. The guide reports status only; it does not enforce the launch gate.
Next: FR-5.2

  • Default state on creation: Given a rep completes the store creation wizard and deploys, when the store is created, then it enters Draft state. The storefront is not live and no buyer-facing URL is accessible.
  • One-way transition: Given a store that has been launched and is now Open, Closed, or Archived, then the Draft state is not available as a target — there is no “revert to draft” action anywhere in the UI.

FR-5.2Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic B — Closing a store (manual)Technical Spec: Store Status & Lifecycle — Epic B
Epic B — Closing a store (manual)
  • As a rep, I want to close my store so buyers can’t place new orders while I make changes, knowing I can re-open it at any time.
  • Trigger surface: Given an Open store, when the rep opens the Store Status card on the Detail tab, then a Close Store action is available. If the store is already Closed, the action is not shown.
  • Confirmation: Given the rep clicks Close Store, when the confirmation appears, then it discloses: buyers won’t be able to place new orders while the store is closed; existing orders are unaffected; the store can be re-opened at any time.
  • Immediate state change: Given the rep confirms, when the action is applied, then the store transitions to Closed instantly — the storefront shows a closed landing page and the status badge updates to Closed.
  • Workspace access retained: Given the store is Closed, when the rep opens any workspace tab (Products, Roster, Orders, Detail), then all content remains fully editable.
  • No order disruption: Given the store is Closed and orders are in-flight, then those orders continue to process normally — no automatic cancellation or hold is applied.
  • Close Report generated: Given the store transitions to Closed by any path, then a Store Close Report is generated for that close event — see the Store Close Report section and Epic H below.
Next: FR-5.3
FR-5.2.1
Screenshot placeholder — Store Status card — Close Store action (Detail tab)
//

FR-5.3Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic C — Auto-close on ordering window close (pop-up stores)Technical Spec: Store Status & Lifecycle — Epic C
Epic C — Auto-close on ordering window close (pop-up stores)
  • As a rep, I want the store to automatically close when its ordering window closes, so I don’t have to manually intervene at end-of-window.
  • Trigger: Given a pop-up store with a configured close date/time, when that moment is reached, then the store automatically transitions to Closed — no rep action required.
  • Behavioral parity: Given the auto-close has fired, then storefront access, order handling, workspace editability, and status badge behavior are identical to a manually closed store.
  • Rep notification: Given auto-close has fired, when the rep next opens the workspace, then a banner or status indicator informs them the ordering window has closed and the store is now Closed. The Store Close Report (Epic H) is the primary presentation of this notification — the report modal replaces the banner as the entry point on first entry after close.
  • Re-open available: Given the auto-closed store, when the rep wants to extend or restart the window, then they can re-open to Open from the Store Status card and optionally edit the store schedule.

Epic D — Re-opening a closed store
  • As a rep, I want to re-open a closed store so my changes go live and buyers can order again.
  • Trigger surface: Given a Closed store, when the rep opens the Store Status card, then a Re-open Store action is available (Close Store is not shown while closed).
  • No confirmation required: Given the rep clicks Re-open Store, then the store transitions to Open immediately — re-opening is a low-risk, reversible action and requires no confirmation modal.
  • Workspace changes go live: Given edits were made in the workspace while Closed, when the store is re-opened, then those changes are reflected on the live storefront immediately.
  • Status badge updates: Given the store is now Open, then the status badge updates to Open in the store list, workspace header, and any KPI aggregations.
  • Prior Close Report retained: Given the store is re-opened, then the Close Report for the previous close event remains viewable on demand and is not overwritten. A subsequent close produces a new, separately scoped report.

FR-5.5Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic E — Archiving a storeTechnical Spec: Store Status & Lifecycle — Epic E
Epic E — Archiving a store
  • As a rep, I want to archive a store at season end so it’s removed from my active list while all history is preserved.
  • See also: Store Detail Tab — Epic C (Store archiving) for the UI surface (Danger Zone placement, modal copy). This epic defines the behavioral contract; that feature owns the controls.
  • Owner-only: Given a store in Open or Closed state, when a rep who is not the owner opens the Detail tab, then the Archive Store action is not visible to them.
  • Confirmation — full consequences disclosed: Given the owning rep clicks Archive Store, when the confirmation modal appears, then it explicitly states all four consequences: (1) the storefront is hidden from all buyers immediately; (2) no new orders can be placed and any active ordering window is closed; (3) the store is removed from the active stores list; (4) order history and reports are retained. It also states that this can only be undone by Prolook support.
  • Immediate storefront takedown: Given the rep confirms, when archiving is applied, then the public storefront URL immediately serves a neutral closed screen and is no longer discoverable or shoppable.
  • Removed from active list: Given the store is Archived, when the rep views the Stores tab, then the store no longer appears in the list.
  • Read-only workspace: Given the store is Archived, when the rep opens the management workspace, then settings, products, roster, and orders are all visible but no field or action is editable.
  • History fully retained: Given the store is Archived, then all order history, reports, roster data, and product records are permanently retained and accessible in the read-only workspace.
  • Support-only reversal: Given the rep wishes to un-archive, when they contact Prolook support, then support can reverse the Archived state — no self-service reversal path exists in the product UI.
Next: FR-5.6

FR-5.6Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic F — Buyer-facing closed storefront screenTechnical Spec: Store Status & Lifecycle — Epic F
Epic F — Buyer-facing closed storefront screen
  • As a buyer, I want a clear message when I land on a closed or archived storefront, so I understand why I can’t order.
  • Closed store: Given a buyer visits a Closed store’s URL, when the page loads, then they see a neutral “This store is currently closed” screen — no products, cart, or checkout are accessible.
  • Archived store: Given a buyer visits an Archived store’s URL, when the page loads, then they see the same neutral closed screen — the storefront is indistinguishable from Closed to buyers; no indication it is permanently archived is surfaced.
  • No leakage: Given either Closed or Archived state, when a buyer attempts to access a product URL or checkout URL directly, then they are redirected to the closed screen — no part of the storefront is accessible via direct link.

FR-5.8Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic H — Store Close ReportTechnical Spec: Store Status & Lifecycle — Epic H
Epic H — Store Close Report
  • As a rep, I want a complete review of everything that happened during the store’s ordering window the moment it closes, so I can verify the numbers, hand off fulfilment, and report back to the team without assembling it myself.
  • As a rep, I want to pull that close report up again later on demand, so I’m not dependent on catching it the first time.
  • Generated on every close event: Given a store transitions to Closed by any path, then a Store Close Report scoped to that Active Order Window is generated and available.
  • Auto-trigger on first entry: Given a Closed store and a user who has not yet seen the report for that close event, when that user next opens the workspace, then the Store Close Report is presented as a prominent full-screen modal before any other workspace content is actionable.
  • Trigger fires once per user per close event: Given the user has viewed or dismissed the report, when they re-enter the workspace, then the modal does not re-appear and the workspace loads in its normal Closed state.
  • Re-close re-arms the trigger: Given a store goes Closed → Open → Closed, when a user next enters the workspace, then the modal fires once more for the new close event, scoped to the new window.
  • Dismissal paths: Given the modal is open, when the user selects View full report, Done, the close icon, Escape, or the backdrop, then the modal closes and the report is marked seen for that user. View full report additionally lands them on the detail layer.
  • On-demand availability: Given a Closed or Archived store, when the user opens the Reports tab, then a Store Close Report entry is present and opens the report in review mode.
  • Unavailable before close: Given a Draft or Open store, when the user opens the Reports tab, then the Store Close Report entry is visible but disabled, with helper text explaining it becomes available when the store closes.
  • Secondary entry point: Given a Closed store, when the rep opens the Store Status card, then a View Close Report link is available alongside Re-open Store.
  • Figures reconcile: Given the report is open, then every figure in the summary layer matches the corresponding figure on the Reports tab for the same window — no figure is computed by a second, divergent method.
  • Complete order coverage: Given the report’s detail layer, then every order placed during the Active Order Window is present, including cancelled and refunded orders, each with its current production stage and fulfilment status.
  • Exports: Given the report is open, when the user exports, then a summary PDF and a full order-level CSV are both available, named per the convention above. No other export format is offered.
  • Team-manager parity: Given a team-manager-role user opens the report, then they can view and export the same content as the rep; owner-only lifecycle actions remain hidden.
  • Retained after re-open and archive: Given a store is re-opened or archived, then previously generated Close Reports remain viewable on demand and their exports continue to work.
  • Skip is always available: Given the summary layer is displayed at any point in the sequence, then a Skip to full report control is visible and moves the user directly to the detail layer.
Next: FR-5.9

FR-5.9Store DefinitionsTechnical Spec: Store Status & Lifecycle — Store Definitions
Store Definitions
  • Status · Detail
  • DRAFT: The default state of every newly created store. The storefront is not live and is inaccessible to buyers. The rep has full management access to configure the store — products, roster, settings, and schedule. A Draft store cannot be launched until at least one product has been added. Once launched it transitions to Open and cannot return to Draft. Draft stores exist only in the management workspace.
  • OPEN: The store is fully operational. The storefront is publicly accessible; buyers can browse, configure, and place new orders. The rep has full management access. This is the state a store enters on launch and returns to when manually re-opened from Closed.
  • CLOSED: The storefront is closed to new orders, but the management workspace remains fully editable and existing orders continue to process normally. Can be entered manually (rep pauses from the Store Status card) or automatically when a pop-up store's configured ordering window close date is reached. The store can be re-opened to Open at any time. Corresponds to the “store closing” state referenced in Store Detail Tab Epics C–D.
  • ARCHIVED: The store is permanently shut down. The storefront is hidden from all buyers immediately; no new orders can be placed; any active ordering window is closed; and the store is removed from the active stores list. All order history and reports are retained as a read-only record. Cannot be self-reversed — restoring an archived store can only be done by a managing rep.
FR-5.10Valid State TransitionsTechnical Spec: Store Status & Lifecycle — Valid State Transitions
Valid State Transitions
  • From > To · Trigger - Who - Reversible?
  • Draft → Open: Manual only — rep clicks Launch Store (requires at least one product to be added first). Rep only. One-way — a store cannot return to Draft once launched.
  • Open → Closed: Manual (rep clicks Pause on the Store Status card) or automatic (scheduled ordering window close date reached). Rep or system. Reversible — rep re-opens to Open at any time.
  • Closed → Open: Manual only — rep clicks Re-open Store on the Store Status card. Rep only. Reversible.
  • Open → Archived: Manual only — store owner clicks Archive Store in the Danger Zone of the Detail tab. Owner only. Irreversible without a support request.
  • Closed → Archived: Manual only — store owner clicks Archive Store in the Danger Zone of the Detail tab. Owner only. Irreversible without a support request.
  • Archived → Open or Closed: Not self-service. Requires a Prolook support request. Support staff only.
  • Open / Closed / Archived → Draft: Not allowed. Draft is the entry state only. Once a store is launched it can never return to Draft.
  • Note: There is no store deletion. Archiving is the terminal state. Draft (pre-launch) is a setup state and not part of the Open / Closed / Archived lifecycle — it transitions to Open on first launch.
FR-5.11Behavior Matrix by SurfaceTechnical Spec: Store Status & Lifecycle — Behavior Matrix by Surface
Behavior Matrix by Surface
  • Surface · Draft · Open · Closed · Archived
  • Storefront (buyer-facing)
    • Draft: Not live — storefront URL is inaccessible to buyers
    • Open: Fully accessible — browse, configure, checkout
    • Closed: “This store is closed” landing page — no shopping or checkout
    • Archived: Hidden immediately — URL serves a neutral closed screen
  • New orders
    • Draft: Blocked — not live
    • Open: Allowed
    • Closed: Blocked
    • Archived: Blocked
  • Existing / in-flight orders
    • Draft: None — store has never been open
    • Open: Unaffected
    • Closed: Unaffected — continue to process normally
    • Archived: Unaffected — retained as read-only history
  • Management workspace
    • Draft: Fully editable
    • Open: Fully editable
    • Closed: Fully editable — changes go live on re-open
    • Archived: Read-only — all records viewable, nothing editable
  • Open stores list
    • Draft: Visible — Draft badge
    • Open: Visible — Open badge
    • Closed: Visible — Closed badge
    • Archived: Removed from list entirely
  • Ordering window (pop-up only)
    • Draft: Configurable but not running
    • Open: Runs as configured
    • Closed: Suspended — does not advance
    • Archived: Closed immediately and permanently
  • Order history & reports
    • Draft: Empty — no orders yet
    • Open: Accessible
    • Closed: Accessible
    • Archived: Retained — read-only
  • Store Close Report
    • Draft: Not applicable — no window has run
    • Open: Not yet generated — becomes available on close
    • Closed: Auto-triggers on first workspace entry after close; available on demand from the Reports tab
    • Archived: Viewable read-only; exports still available
  • Overview KPI — Open Stores count
    • Draft: Not counted
    • Open: Counted
    • Closed: Not counted
    • Archived: Not counted
FR-5.12Store Close ReportTechnical Spec: Store Status & Lifecycle — Store Close Report
Store Close Report
  • The moment a store closes is the one moment a rep needs the whole window summarized in one place — and today nothing does that. The rep is left assembling the picture manually across the Reports tab, the Orders tab, and the Fundraising Report. The Store Close Report closes that gap and becomes the opening step of the store-closing flow.
  • This section defines the behavioral contract for the report. The Reports tab owns the on-demand entry point and the report UI itself; the Store Status card owns the secondary link.
Definition & scope
  • The Store Close Report is a complete review of every order placed during the store’s Active Order Window (open date → close date), plus summarized performance data for that window. It serves two jobs at once:
  • Summary layer: a card-paced, progressively revealed recap of the window’s headline numbers, designed to be read in under a minute and shared with the team or league.
  • Detail layer: the full order-level operational record the rep verifies, reconciles, and exports for fulfilment.
  • The report is generated per close event. A close event is any transition into Closed, whether manual (Close Store) or automatic (ordering window close date reached). A store that is re-opened and later closed again produces a new close event with a new report scoped to the new window; prior reports are retained.
  • Note: The Close Report is a read layer only. It introduces no new metrics, no new state, and no changes to the lifecycle state machine, notifications, or the buyer-facing closed storefront screen. Every figure it shows is sourced from — and must reconcile exactly with — the Reports tab and the Orders tab.
Trigger & presentation
  • Auto-trigger condition: the store is in Closed state and the current user has not yet seen the report for this close event.
  • Presentation: a prominent full-screen modal (a dedicated full-page route at narrow viewport widths) shown on first workspace entry after close. It kicks off the closing flow and serves as the presentation of the Epic C rep notification. The summary layer uses the shared paced card sequence shell specified in System Modals & Toasts — this section owns the card payload and the trigger, not the shell.
  • Seen state is per user: A rep and an invited team-manager-role user each see the auto-trigger once for the same close event.
  • Dismissal: primary action View full report (opens the detail layer); secondary Done / close icon; Escape key and backdrop click also dismiss. Any dismissal marks the report as seen for that user.
  • Subsequent visits: no auto-trigger. The workspace shows the normal Closed state and the report is reachable on demand.
  • Re-close: Closed → Open → Closed re-arms the auto-trigger once for the new close event.
  • Always-open (non-pop-up) stores: no scheduled close date exists, so the auto-trigger only fires after a manual Close Store.
  • Archived stores: the report remains viewable on demand as part of the read-only record; no auto-trigger fires.
On-demand access
  • Primary entry point — Reports tab: A Store Close Report entry sits at the top of the Reports tab. It is available when the store is Closed or Archived; for Draft and Open stores it renders disabled with helper text (“Available when the store closes”).
  • Secondary entry point — Store Status card: A View Close Report link sits alongside the Close Store / Re-open Store actions, so the report is reachable from the same card that governs the transition.
  • Review mode: Opening on demand lands on the summary layer with all controls visible and no forced pacing, with the detail layer one click away. The user is never trapped in the paced sequence.
Report contents
  • Layer · Contents
  • Summary layer (hero treatment, one figure per view): Total Revenue · Orders · Net Fundraising (only when Team Fundraising is enabled; Avg. Order Value is promoted into this slot when it is disabled) · one derived superlative — top product by quantity ordered. Each figure carries its exact value, its unit, and an “as of [close date]” stamp, plus the vs.-last-period trend delta the KPI cards already use.
  • Detail layer (tables and charts): Full order table scoped to the window, at line-item grain: Web Order #, Factory Order #, order origin badge, buyer, roster / team, product, size, quantity, unit price, line total, personalization (name / number), production stage, PO number, tracking, fulfilment status, and any Minimum Quantity Fee — with the existing Orders toolbar (search, sort, filter, date range, column visibility, row selection). Plus the Team Profit chart for the window; Pending Orders count and any below-MOQ containers still open at close; the Fundraising Report breakdown (Fundraising Collected, Platform Fees, Card Processing Fees, Net Fundraising); Avg. Order Value; the production-stage mix (Order placed → Confirmed → Artwork → Factory production → Shipped → Delivered); and a refunds / cancellations summary.
  • Refunds and cancellations net out of revenue consistently with the Reports tab, and the netting is stated on the report so the summary and detail figures always reconcile.
Exports
  • Summary PDF: a presentation-ready one-pager carrying store name, Team Logo and Team Colors, the Active Order Window dates, the hero figures, and the Team Profit trend. Follows the existing Fundraising Report Download PDF pattern, including a settings step (summary style, detailed breakdown, selected date range, report notes).
  • Full order-level CSV: every order in the window at line-item grain, following the existing All Orders CSV Export pattern and respecting the current column-visibility selection.
  • PDF and CSV are the only export formats: No image or share-card export is in scope.
  • File naming: StoreCloseReport_[StoreName]_[CloseDate].pdf and StoreCloseReport_[StoreName]_[CloseDate].csv.
Roles & permissions
  • Rep / store owner: full access to the report and both exports.
  • Team-manager role: full parity with the rep for this report — a team-manager-role user may view and export everything the report contains. Owner-only lifecycle actions (notably Archive Store, per Epic E) remain hidden and are unaffected.
  • No separate dealer or decorator view is required.
Experience principles
  • The report is data-dense but must read as accessible and familiar rather than as a spreadsheet — the reference points are Apple Music Replay and Spotify Wrapped, adapted with restraint for an operator who has to trust the numbers.
  • Step down, don’t wall off: Cover → three to five hero figures → a full recap card → a clear handoff into the detail tables. A persistent Skip to full report control and a progress indicator are visible at all times.
  • User-paced: One figure per view, advanced by click, tap, or arrow keys. No auto-advance.
  • Restrained motion: Fades and scale-ins around 200–300ms; a count-up on the hero figure only. No parallax or confetti, and no looping animation on a figure card — motion must never compete with a number the rep is being asked to trust. Motion is otherwise specified once for both paced card sequences in System Modals & Toasts; there is no in-product reduce-motion control.
  • Three type levels only: hero figure, section header, label — with tabular lining numerals throughout so figures align and do not jitter during count-up.
  • Store-owned palette: The report uses the store’s configured Team Colors and Team Logo. Color communicates status (trend up / down) rather than decoration.
  • Trustworthy by default: Every hero view shows the exact figure and its as-of date. Celebratory framing is suppressed wherever the data does not support it.
FR-5.13Edge casesTechnical Spec: Store Status & Lifecycle — Edge cases
Edge cases
  • Case · Behavior
  • Zero orders in the window: Superlatives and celebratory framing are suppressed. The report shows a calm factual summary of the window and its dates, and the detail layer shows an empty state. Exports still produce valid files with headers and zero rows.
  • Very low order count (single order): The top-product superlative degrades to that one order and is phrased so it does not imply a ranking that does not exist.
  • Closed with unfulfilled / in-production orders: The summary layer still reports window totals. The detail layer surfaces a prominent count of orders not yet shipped alongside the production-stage mix, reinforcing that existing orders continue to process after close.
  • Closed with unsettled payment (open PO): Affected orders are flagged in the detail table and summarized in a needs-attention strip. Framing is factual, not alarmist.
  • Refunds and cancellations present: A refunds / cancellations summary (count and amount) appears in the detail layer, netted against revenue consistently with the Reports tab, with the netting stated on the report.
  • Below-MOQ containers still open at close: Listed in the detail layer with their MOQ progress and any itemized Minimum Quantity Fee, so the rep can see what did not reach minimum before the window closed.
  • Very large stores: The summary layer is fixed size regardless of order volume. The detail table paginates and virtualizes; exports still contain the complete data set.
  • Team Fundraising disabled: The Net Fundraising hero figure and the fundraising breakdown are omitted entirely; Avg. Order Value is promoted into the vacated hero slot.
FR-5.14Metrics & Success CriteriaTechnical Spec: Store Status & Lifecycle — Metrics & Success Criteria
Metrics & Success Criteria
  • Launch gate enforcement: 0% of stores successfully launch with zero products
  • Draft-to-launch conversion rate (signal for setup friction)
  • Auto-close accuracy: 100% of pop-up stores transition to Closed at their configured close date/time
  • Zero buyer orders accepted while a store is in Closed or Archived state
  • Close → re-open cycle time (signal for how quickly reps act after a window closes)
  • Archive rate per season (adoption of intentional lifecycle management vs. stores abandoned in Open)
  • Support tickets for archive reversal (informs whether a self-service undo path is needed)
  • Close Report generation reliability: 100% of close events produce a viewable report
  • Close Report engagement rate on first workspace entry after close (share of auto-triggered modals viewed rather than immediately dismissed)
  • Share of close events where the report is exported as PDF or CSV (signal for whether it is replacing manual assembly)
  • On-demand Close Report views per closed store (signal for whether the report is being returned to, not just seen once)
  • Skip-to-full-report rate (signal for whether the summary layer earns its place ahead of the detail tables)
  • Reduction in manual Reports-tab + Orders-tab + Fundraising Report sessions in the 48 hrs following a close
FR-5.15Dependencies & Technical ConstraintsTechnical Spec: Store Status & Lifecycle — Dependencies & Technical Constraints
Dependency & Technical Constraint
  • Verification is a manual, asynchronous process — not an automated check. Requires: secure database storage for application submissions (business-entity fields incl. EIN and banking, with sensitive-data handling/encryption); an internal admin review queue and tooling plus a notification service for the Prolook reviewer; and a persistent access-grant/entitlement model (not session-only). Approval introduces a 24–48 hr waiting period between submission and access. Likely third-party integrations for banking/EIN validation and applicant email notification.
Store Close Report — dependencies
  • Requires a per-user, per-close-event seen-state record (not session-only) so the auto-trigger fires exactly once per user per close event and survives re-close. Requires a durable close-event record capturing the window’s open and close timestamps and the trigger source (rep name or “system” for auto-close, consistent with System Emails), so a re-opened-and-re-closed store produces correctly scoped, non-overwriting reports. Report aggregation must read from the same source as the Reports tab KPI cards and the Fundraising Report rather than recomputing, or the summary and detail layers will diverge. PDF generation follows the existing Fundraising Report export service; CSV follows the All Orders export service. The summary layer's shell is the shared paced card sequence component specified in System Modals & Toasts.
FR-6Order Management (Orders Tab)
  • The Problem: A rep running a team store has orders coming from three directions — their own rep-placed orders, individual player orders for team-required/supplied gear, and fan orders — and no single place to see them. Two operational realities make this acute: most manufacturers enforce minimum order quantities (MOQs), so submitting too early incurs large fees; and reps must know which rostered players are still missing required gear before deadlines.
  • The Solution: An Orders tab inside the store manager that consolidates order activity into two sub-tabs — All Orders and Pending Orders. Pending Orders is built around the MOQ workflow (let orders pile up, then submit in batches with the minimum-quantity fee surfaced); the per-player ordered-vs-missing view lives in the My Team(s) tab as a player drawer. Any order expands into a side-drawer order detail.
  • Audit update — July 16, 2026: this spec was audited against the current Team Stores Workspace prototype and updated to match its behavior (two sub-tabs, All Orders toolbar, roster-based pending containers, review-modal fee handling, freight-routing gate, and per-player tracking relocated to the My Team(s) tab).
  • Terminology update — September 11, 2026: the Roster tab has been renamed the My Team(s) tab. All references to the tab in this spec now use the new name; references to rosters as a data concept (roster groups, standalone rosters, roster architecture) are unchanged.
  • Amendment — October 13, 2026: the order line snapshots personalization at purchase and is never re-read from the roster. A new Governing rule states it, and Epic E specifies the one narrow path by which a pending order line may be updated to match a changed roster. This exists because roster edits are deliberately unrestricted — see Roster Management (My Team(s) Tab), Epics 2 and 4 — and the snapshot is what makes that safe.
  • Who is this for? The store manager (rep) viewing and managing all orders in their team store — their own orders, player orders, and fan orders.
  • Scenarios encountered: Letting pending orders accumulate to hit a factory MOQ before submitting; submitting a shipping group / selected / all pending orders; submitting below MOQ with the minimum-quantity fee itemized in the review modal; entering PO payment + shipping and reviewing items before final submit; opening a player's drawer in the My Team(s) tab to spot missing required gear and send a reminder; correcting a jersey number on the roster and pushing that correction onto the order before it goes to the factory; discovering after submission that a player's ordered number no longer matches the roster, and warning the parent rather than changing anything; opening any order to inspect its full detail.
FR-6.1Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsUser StoriesTechnical Spec: Order Management (Orders Tab) — Functional Requirements & Core Workflows
User Stories
  • As a rep, I want every order in my store — mine, players', and fans' — manageable from one Orders tab, so that I can manage fulfillment from a single place.
  • As a rep, I want to let pending orders accumulate and submit them in batches, so that I hit factory MOQs and avoid below-minimum fees.
  • As a rep, I want each shipping group's progress to MOQ and the exact fee for submitting short surfaced up front and itemized at review, so that I never incur a fee by accident.
  • As a rep, I want a per-player view of ordered vs. missing required gear in the My Team(s) tab, so that I can quickly chase down players who haven't ordered.
  • As a rep, I want an order to keep the details it was placed with, so that tidying up a roster never changes what the factory makes.
  • As a rep who corrects a number before anything is submitted, I want to push that correction onto the order, so that the right jersey gets made.
  • As a rep, I want to expand any order into a detail drawer, so that I can inspect items, status, pricing, and shipping.
Next: FR-6.2
FR-6.2Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic A — Orders tab & sub-tabsTechnical Spec: Order Management (Orders Tab) — Epic A
Epic A — Orders tab & sub-tabs
  • The Orders page presents two sub-tabs: All Orders (default) and Pending Orders, which carries a live count badge. There is no Player Orders sub-tab — per-player order tracking lives in the My Team(s) tab (Epic C). A search bar (by name, email, or order number) filters within the active sub-tab.
  • All Orders (default): Given the Orders tab, when it opens, then All Orders is shown by default, consolidating the rep's own orders and fan orders into one view, with each row badged by origin (My Order / Fan). Pending orders appear only in the Pending sub-tab.
  • All Orders toolbar & table: the view carries search, sort, filter, a date-range picker, a column-visibility control, row selection with a bulk bar (Export Selected), pagination, and a CSV Export. An Order Status Guide explains each lifecycle stage, and the factory-order-number link opens the detail drawer directly in Order View.
  • Pending Orders: Given the Pending sub-tab, then it lists orders that have been placed in the storefront but not yet approved by the rep and sent to the factory.

FR-6.3Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic B — Pending Orders & MOQ submission (High-impact)Technical Spec: Order Management (Orders Tab) — Epic B
Epic B — Pending Orders & MOQ submission (High-impact)
  • Pending orders are grouped into containers that mirror the store's roster architecture: League stores render one container per roster group plus one per standalone roster; Single-Team stores get a single container. Each container header shows the team code and rosters, its shipping destination (league / group / team managers), and — when below minimum — a minimum-quantity progress ring with the % to MOQ and an info tooltip stating the fee that applies if submitted short. This workflow matters because reps deliberately let orders pile up until they hit MOQ to avoid large below-minimum fees.
  • Selection & submit scope: Given the pending list, the rep can Submit Group from any container header, check any number of rows and Submit Selected (n), or Submit All from the sticky footer bar.
  • Below-MOQ handling: a below-MOQ submission proceeds into the same review modal, with the Minimum Quantity Fee itemized per shipping group and in the order summary — the rep sees exactly what the shortfall costs before confirming. There is no separate destructive-confirmation step.
  • Review & submit modal: Given any submission, then a modal opens where the rep reviews shipping details, enters payment as a PO Number, confirms billing, and reviews the order summary (subtotal, any minimum-quantity fee, total) and the items grouped by shipping group (thumbnail, size, quantity, price) before clicking Confirm · $total.
  • Freight-routing gate: Given a submission that includes a group whose fulfillment routing is unresolved (missing group/team manager), then an Assign Managers modal blocks the flow — collecting the group manager, or a team manager for every unassigned roster, with address verification — and on completion automatically continues to the review modal.
  • Submission closes the pending window: The moment an order is submitted it becomes a factory instruction: its personalization snapshot is final and the Epic E update is withdrawn. Nothing in the review modal re-reads the roster, so a roster edit made while the modal is open does not alter what is being submitted.
Next: FR-6.4

FR-6.4Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic C — Player order tracking (My Team(s) tab) (High-importance)Technical Spec: Order Management (Orders Tab) — Epic C
Epic C — Player order tracking (My Team(s) tab) (High-importance)
  • Per-player order tracking lives in the My Team(s) tab, not an Orders sub-tab: each rostered player's drawer aggregates all of their team-required / team-supplied gear.
  • Given a player's drawer, then each required/supplied item shows an ordered state (size, quantity, order number, order date, status badge, and price — $0.00 for team-supplied gear) or a red "Not ordered" flag, so the rep can immediately see which players are missing required gear.
  • Given a player missing required items, then a Send Reminder action appears pinned to the drawer footer (only while gear is still un-ordered).
  • Divergence is reported in this drawer: Given an ordered item whose snapshotted personalization no longer matches the player's current roster values, then the item states both — ordered as #12 · roster says #7 — and, while the order is still pending, carries the Epic E update action. The display rules are owned by Roster Management, Epic 2; the underlying comparison and the update are owned here.
  • Divergence does not change completion: The item was ordered and remains ordered, so it still counts toward the player's team-gear completion and does not re-arm Send Reminder.

FR-6.5Functional Requirements & Core WorkflowsEpic D — Order detail drawerTechnical Spec: Order Management (Orders Tab) — Epic D
Epic D — Order detail drawer
  • Given any order in any sub-tab, when the rep clicks it, then a side drawer slides in with the full order detail — product art, status with a production-stage stepper (Submit Date → Order Confirmed → production stages → Delivery), quantity / sport / PO / tracking, delivery, rep/dealership, and item pricing — toggleable between Order View and Item View.
  • View behavior: the drawer header carries the Web Order # and Factory Order #, organization, and cart name; it opens in Item View by default (or Order View when opened from a factory-order link). Order View shows the whole web order — summary cells plus grouped line items — and clicking a line item jumps to that item's Item View.
  • Item View shows the snapshot, and says so where it has drifted: Personalization displayed in the drawer is the order line's own recorded values, never the player's current record. Where the two differ, Item View notes the current roster value alongside the ordered one, so a rep inspecting an order is never left guessing which number the garment carries.
